### Mystic canonical owners — 2026-09-20

- `eVe/intuition/menu/context_menus.json`, `eVe/intuition/menu/context_menus_loader.js` and `eVe/intuition/menu/context_menu_resolver.js`: validated composition — the Mystic is one constant ordered list (`menus.mystic.fixed`, read by `resolveFixedMysticMenu`; `utilities` is its palette and replaces `mode` there), reduced outside edit to the two switches of the work mode (`menus.mystic.modes`: consultation = `perform` + `mode_edit`, performance = `mode_edit` + `mode_consume`), the lasso and an armed tool keep their own tables, and `menus.main.roots` drives the permanent bar.
- `eVe/intuition/runtime/eve_intuition/main_menu_content_runtime.js` owns the `utilities` palette (`mode`/`validation`/`matrix`, no tool redefined); `eVe/intuition/runtime/eve_intuition/mystic_context_items_runtime.js` renders those children and routes the project background's `play` to `ui.project.transport`; `eVe/intuition/mystic/context.js` owns the released-pointer rule (no dwell after a release, `dwell: false`).
- `eVe/voice/assistant/assistant_dock.js`: corner controls over the standard Atom tile and existing text-session composition; shared main-menu hold recognition controls text visibility.
- `atome/src/squirrel/voice/realtime_session.js`: authoritative acquired microphone-track state.
- `eVe/intuition/runtime/bevy_panel/bevy_panel_home_view.js`: explicit stored-provider activation via the existing vault runtime.
- `eVe/domains/rendering/project_work_mode_state.js`: session project modes and transition preservation boundary.
- `eVe/intuition/mystic/mystic_menu_items.js`: configured anchors and pagination.
- `eVe/intuition/ribbon/bevy_ui_mystic_runtime.js`: pointer, submenu, page and closure lifecycle.

The implementation/acceptance report lists remaining gaps: eVe/documentations/MYSTIC_VALIDATION_2026-09-20.md.


### Nested crop and source-space media projection — 2026-09-17

- `surface_interaction_runtime.js` also serializes the asynchronous `select` + `atome.edit.enter` transition behind the next pointer, wheel, or native gesture. An immediate pinch/Ctrl-wheel after a video or audio double-click therefore enters `spatial_crop`/`temporal_crop` before generic resize can start.
- `atome_contextual_kind.js` reuses the media playback normalizer so persisted `video_recording` and `audio_recording` kinds enter the existing video/audio crop owners; photo/picture aliases converge on image. Inline SVG RenderAtoms enter the existing `svg_vector_edit_runtime.js` instead of being rejected by the media-only double-click filter.
- `atome_contextual_edit_runtime.js` keeps an exact edited descendant active when selection changes inside an edited Molecule; an ancestor group cannot reclaim an already active media crop session.
- `surface_interaction_runtime.js` owns recursive open-Molecule target resolution; it exposes a raw descendant when any ancestor remains in contextual edition and otherwise resolves the closed structural root. Mystic consumes the same resolver.
- `bevy_media_texture_resolver.js` keeps cropped images in natural source aspect and waveforms in full-peak source coordinates, reusing the bounded shared texture cache.
- `render_atom.js` and `bevy_renderer_adapter_registry.js` derive image/video and temporal waveform `uv_rect`; Bevy core `spawn.rs`/`resource_ops.rs` apply it and keep `AtomeSpriteSourceRect` synchronized through clipping.

### Contextual crop and footer removal — 2026-09-17

- Crop math and bounded property projection: `eVe/domains/rendering/media_crop_model.js`.
- Natural canvas gestures: `surface_interaction_runtime.js` and `surface_pinch_runtime.js`; Structured visual gestures: `project_view_visual_interaction_runtime.js`.
- Disposable preview and terminal commit: `project_scene_engine.js`.
- Image/video `source_rect` projection: `render_atom.js`, `bevy_projection_media_contract.js`, `bevy_renderer_adapter_registry.js`, and Bevy core `texture.rs`/`spawn.rs`/`resource_ops.rs`.
- Temporal waveform/playback: `project_view_list_preview_model.js`, `project_audio_playback_progress_runtime.js`, `selected_project_media_playback_runtime.js`, `media_playback_audio_executor.js`.
- Contextual UI now owns rail/vector nodes only: `atome_contextual_edit_model.js`, `atome_contextual_edit_runtime.js`, `atome_contextual_edit_handlers.js`; `atome_contextual_fullscreen_runtime.js` is deleted.
- `surface_interaction_runtime.js` preserves canonical `group`/`molecule` routing independently of projected `shape`; `media_crop_model.js` owns fit-derived image source windows and `surface_pinch_runtime.js` owns terminal pinch commit/cancel lifecycle.

### Canvas lasso Delete and iOS Molecule parentage — 2026-09-16

`eVe/intuition/runtime/eve_intuition/atome_contextual_rail_definition_invocation_runtime.js` distinguishes a structured List/Matrix rail (`record.structured_context`) from the Natural canvas rail. Structured Delete remains bound to its active row; canvas Delete forwards the complete canonical selection, including every invisible Molecule owner selected by a lasso. `eVe/intuition/tools/core/tool_runtime_molecule_structure.js` remains the whole-owner deletion authority. Native iOS event projection persists structural `parent_id`; derived `molecule_timeline` clips are not membership authority and must not keep an empty Molecule alive.

### Molecule-source composition and contextual Ungroup — 2026-09-15

`surface_text_pointer_runtime.js` records the number of gesture sources separately from `surface_pointer_runtime.js` expanding a single Molecule to its descendants for collective motion. `project_view_drop_feedback.js`, `surface_interaction_runtime.js`, and `project_scene_engine.js` carry that source count through the existing held composition palette and terminal `drag.end`, so one Molecule source can use all six choices without enabling composition for a genuine multi-selection. The result still delegates to `combineCanonicalMolecule`, which preserves the source Molecule envelope and nests it inside the new wrapper. Mystic continues to resolve closed descendants to their Molecule owner; its existing `ungroup` capability invokes `ungroupCanonicalMolecule`, with the French presentation labelled « Dégrouper ».

### Natural project selection closes the Atome rail — 2026-09-15

`eVe/core/atome_events/project_layer_routing.js` keeps its existing responsibility of publishing the project id as the canonical replacement selection. `eVe/intuition/runtime/eve_intuition/atome_contextual_edit_runtime.js` remains the sole consumer that owns Natural contextual lifetime: when the replacement selection does not resolve to an Atome in the active Natural scene, it removes stale selection-level context and deactivates the rail while preserving explicit edition sessions. No second selection listener or UI owner is introduced.

### Gesture, lasso and history owners — 2026-09-14

- `eVe/domains/rendering/surface_pointer_runtime.js` owns proportional target geometry and shared size limits. `eVe/domains/rendering/surface_pinch_runtime.js` owns multi-contact/wheel lifecycle only; surface, contextual footer and Size inputs reuse the shared calculation. Invisible edge hit-testing and object-footer resize grips are removed.
- `eVe/shared/lasso_context_zone_runtime.js` retains only the waiting rectangle lifetime and fixed context. The official Mystic context owner and shared Bevy Mystic consume Text, Capture, Molecule, Shape, Copy, Paste and Delete descriptors; the temporary menu support module and its buttons are removed. Capture reuses `eVe/shared/lasso_context_capture.js` and the shared Bevy project-preview adapter, including crop origin.
- `eVe/core/atome_commit_state.js` tracks pending mutation completion without another history. Scene mutations converge on one batch commit; `eVe/core/atome_timeline.js` serializes history actions after this barrier and resolves the remaining targets of partially undone transactions. `server/atomeHistoryCommands.js` applies optional selection IDs inside the source transaction and uses the canonical history classifier to exclude replay-only gesture frames from inversion; `server/atomePropertySecurity.js` preserves readable gesture completion and history source metadata.
- `eVe/intuition/mystic/context_pointer_lock.js` announces pointer takeover when Mystic opens. The existing DOM drag and shared WebGPU surface owners consume that signal to release their sessions immediately while selection remains canonical. `copy.js` accepts Mystic's captured selection, while `paste.js` delegates Atome cloning and internal-reference remapping to the existing duplicate owner.
- `eVe/intuition/runtime/eve_intuition/main_menu_edit_content.js` shares edit definitions between menu consumers; Redo delegates directly to existing `ui.redo` without a self-invoking touch callback.

OpenAI attachment ownership: assistant_media_session retains explicit files via asset_box_upload_transport.sendFileToServer and asset_box_atome_store.createUploadAtome, then records references through conversation_store. asset_box_file_upload.js owns the extracted file upload protocol (312 lines before lifecycle additions), reducing asset_box_upload_transport.js to 341 lines and exposing upload without loading the legacy uploads UI. Existing remote-wallpaper behavior remains in its original owner and passes its probe. conversation_store serializes saves and rejects account changes/deleted records. Finder closes normally after resuming a saved discussion.


OpenAI acceptance owners: tests/probes/openai_assistant_ui_acceptance.mjs reuses the canonical canvas harness for real held-pointer, text-focus and level-drag checks. assistant_scene.js anchors the procedural record to main_menu_tool_atome_icon_image; assistant_visual_contract.js converts top-origin scene coordinates to the existing bottom-origin material coordinates. auth_users.authenticatedUserSnapshot owns private self-authentication projection.
### OpenAI integration owners — 2026-09-10 (acceptance in progress)

- Native transport: Rust `platforms/desktop-tauri/src/server/provider_relay.rs` binds provider sockets to current remote account credentials; `platforms/desktop-tauri/src/server/ws_api.rs` owns extracted WS API dispatch. iOS `FastifySyncClient.swift` owns the equivalent relay invoked by `LocalHTTPServer.swift`. No primary OpenAI key is returned to native clients.
- Server: `providerHostedSession.js` owns connection-bound, one-use Remote MCP approvals. `providerCredentialVault.js` adapts the existing encrypted token vault; `wsAiProviderOperations.js` dispatches authenticated provider requests in the existing socket. `providerRealtimeSession.js` owns the separate lifetime of the GPT-Live sideband sockets and their `session.close`, not Atome mutations; `providerRealtimeSpeech.js` owns speech synthesis.
- Server: `providerSharedGrant.js` (2026-10-18) owns the bounded, revocable test seats on the platform OpenAI key: tables `provider_shared_seats`, `provider_shared_usage` and `provider_shared_settings` in `database/auth_security_schema.sql`; `wsAiProviderOperations.js` asks it only when the principal's vault has no own key (`models` and the main OpenAI route, never `audio.*`, `video.*`, `music.*` or `social.*`). Operator tool: `scripts/ai_shared_key_admin.mjs` (list, find, grant, revoke, unblock, close, open). Regression owner: `tests/server/provider_shared_grant.test.mjs`.
- Server: `billing/` (2026-10-18, lot 1 of `todo/offre_unique_stockage_ia.md`) owns the single offer. `atome/src/shared/billing_contract.js` (the former server-only catalog, shared since 2026-10-18) holds the plan tiers and credit packs (product ids shared by App Store, Google Play and Stripe), the simulated Web methods, the store of each platform and `billingAccountToken`; `billing_ledger.js` owns purchases, derived entitlements and the append-only credit ledger (tables `billing_purchases`, `billing_credit_ledger` in `database/auth_security_schema.sql`); `ws_billing_operations.js` answers the `billing` family. `auth_accounts.js` and `auth_phone_link.js` record the still-simulated creation payment through `applySimulatedPlan`. `wsApiOperationChain.js` now owns the ordered list of authenticated `/ws/api` families that reply-or-null (external resources, ai-provider, billing, atome, surface, teleport, surface grants), removed from `server.js`. Regression owner: `tests/server/billing_ledger.test.mjs`.
- In-app purchase (2026-10-18): `server/billing/apple_store_verify.js` (StoreKit 2 JWS), `google_play_verify.js` (Play Developer API, acknowledge/consume, RTDN parsing), `store_purchases.js` (verify, bind to the account by original transaction or device-key account token, creation payment `prepareCreation`, store notifications), `billing_routes.js` (the two store HTTP endpoints, registered in `server.js`). `auth_accounts.js` and `auth_phone_link.js#pay` take `billing.prepareCreation`. Client: `atome/src/squirrel/billing/store_purchase.js` (host resolution and `creationPayment`, shared by `auth_pairing_client.js` and `auth_phone_link_client.js`). First launch: `first_launch_billing_store` in `eVe/domains/templates/first_launch_template_catalog.js` (offer, price, « Payer », « Retour », no Web method) chosen by `first_launch_runtime.js` when `AdoleAPI.auth.paymentStore()` names a store. Native: `platforms/ios/atome-auv3/Common/PurchaseManager.swift` (command `billing_store_purchase` in `WebViewManagerIPC.swift`), `platforms/desktop-tauri/src/native_billing.rs` + `vendor/tauri-plugin-billing` (`BillingPlugin.kt`). Tests: `tests/server/billing_store.test.mjs` (fixture chain `tests/server/fixtures/billing/`), `tests/eve/billing_store_purchase.test.mjs`.
- Shared AI: `provider_broker.js` consumes the existing authenticated transport; `openai_stream.js` decodes SSE and owns `readResponseText` (output_text of a Responses result). `conversation_session.js` owns temporary public turns, bounded MCP sequences and pending decisions; `conversation_store.js` persists typed conversations through canonical Atome APIs. Finder consumes those records.
- Media/UI: `realtime_session.js` owns duplex capture/connection lifecycle. `tts_runtime.js` replaces `local_tts_runtime.js` with one PCM playback owner for local and OpenAI synthesis. `assistant_media_session.js` owns temporary references/results; `svg_draw_mask_runtime.js` composes existing Drawing geometry and shared project export. Assistant views consume main-menu inline controls and shared panel primitives.


### Dashboard, List and capture repairs — 2026-09-10

`eVe/domains/dashboard/dashboard_layout.js` and `dashboard_records.js` project the creation grid of the active header (`dashboard_creation_catalog.js` / `dashboard_creation_runtime.js` / `dashboard_creation_records.js`). `dashboard_creation_actions.js` creates through the existing project and News commands. News template vocabulary remains owned by `news_template_model.js`.

`project_view_list_content.js` owns transient name expansion. The shared selectable-list drag session recognizes hold release and cancels that interpretation after a drag. Surface mount/unmount clear expansion on project changes, including a project opened in Natural. `text_editing_layout.js` fits compact labels without changing font size. `bevy_ui_scroll_layout.js` now owns both content extents and scrollbar geometry; `bevy_ui_scroll_runtime.js` owns gestures and offsets.

`eVe/intuition/tools/capture_video_preview_panel.js` consumes the standard panel host and `bevy_panel_record_preview.js`. It is the camera preview of a video take and of a photo session: no title, footer, close button or hierarchy rail; `fullscreenBody` + `hideFooter` give it the whole surface (square shell) and `belowMainMenu` stacks it under the ribbon, whose red rec. tool stops the session; the contextual rail is suspended through its own `setSuspended` while it is open. A photo session (`eVe/intuition/tools/capture_photo_session_runtime.js`, `ui.capture.photo` toggled from the ribbon or the beginner rail) opens the camera through `openCameraPreview` (`eVe/domains/media/api/video_api_preview.js`: browser `getUserMedia`, iOS `media_camera_preview_start/frame/stop` on `AppNativeMediaCaptureController`, whose photo output then takes every still from the running session); each touch in the preview invokes `ui.capture.photo` with `shot: true`; a capture aimed at a target (placeholder, Mystic home `ownerProjectId`) stays one shot. It shows a live camera as a `video` record decoded by Bevy under `CAPTURE_VIDEO_PREVIEW_RECORD_ID` (`registerBevyVideoStreamSource`, 30 fps cap for this source): no CPU readback, no per-frame panel rebuild. On iOS the native recorder has no WebView `MediaStream`: `eVe/domains/media/api/video_native_preview_stream.js` turns its JPEG preview frames (960 px, 30 fps, `AppNativeVideoRecorderPreview.swift`) into one through `canvas.captureStream(0)` + `requestFrame()`, so every runtime takes the same route. The recording API retains acquisition and stop ownership. Pointer fixtures now compose live shared controls instead of importing the retired Panel Lab.


### Context scope lifetime — 2026-09-09

`project_view_surface_context_runtime` retains project/navigation identity and context revision through owner resolution and invocation. Old navigation cannot create a container rail under a new project ID; late reads and retained closures become stale. Real Dashboard cross-project return and 15 context/readiness regressions verify this owner.

### Performance sample boundaries — 2026-09-09

`project_view_capture_to_timeline.clipOperation` quantizes measured start/end through existing `time_model` conversion and derives duration from their frame difference. `kernel/collisions` compares sample boundaries using the owning timeline sample rate, supplied by reducers, stretch and block loop. This prevents an artificial overlap between adjacent captured holds, including floating-second addition error. No tolerance, new timebase, clip displacement or alternate recording owner is added.

### Audio upload ownership — 2026-09-09

`createAudioStorage` in `eVe/domains/media/api/audio_core_storage.js` owns `uploadRecordingToFastify`. Recording finalization and durable queue replay consume it through the existing shared audio context. Failed queued upload propagates to the existing facade reporter and does not mark the job done. The write-only in-flight flag and duplicate upload ownership were removed; headers, credentials and upload bytes are preserved. Storage/flush regression: 15 tests passed; final M0/Molecule: 28 suites passed.

### Composition owner convergence — 2026-09-09

- project_view_drop_feedback composes the shared tool factory and popup placement for six explicit choices; it resolves the terminal release point as the sole valid choice. The original Natural/List/Matrix session continues moving the dragged source beneath the higher-depth projection. Beyond the 20 px outer-bound margin, the existing projection becomes empty and non-selectable but stays mounted until the pointer session ends; this avoids a transient surface resize from terminating a held drag when another stacked Atome is revealed. The same tree is refreshed for a later stationary target, while the already armed target prevents immediate rearming until it is left. Existing gesture sessions own the single final cleanup, and an outside release cannot reuse a previously hovered choice.
- tool_runtime_molecule_combine delegates cuts to project_view_occurrence_edit_runtime and copies to planCanonicalRecordCopies in tool_runtime_atome_duplicate. Group windows are compiled by project_view_transport_plan; canonical parentage uses atome_record_utils.resolveAtomeParentId.
- tool_runtime_molecule_structure exposes the existing transform planner for translating whole nested source envelopes.

### Dashboard and shared interaction owners — 2026-09-09

- Dashboard creation mode (2026-09-18): a SHORT press on the lower third of a creatable header (`projects`, `news`, `calendar`, `contacts`) replaces every card lane with a full-height grid of creation cells drawn with the card glass; a press on the lower band of ANY header (active or dimmed) closes it. `dashboard_creation_catalog.js` owns the steps (project families/goals, News shapes and the custom view/content steps, calendar/contact editor cells), `dashboard_creation_runtime.js` the path/toggle/busy state (`state.activeCategoryId` + `state.creation`), `dashboard_layout.js#layoutDashboardCreation` the grid (anchored to the header side, shrinks to fit), `dashboard_creation_records.js` the cells, and `dashboard_creation_actions.js` creates through the canonical project/News creators then opens through `actions.openProjectItem`, or opens the registered `calendar`/`contact` editor. Non-concerned headers are drawn at 0.35 opacity. The upper two thirds of the same header belong to the category filter (see the two-tier rail entry), so creation and filter stay exclusive. There is no header long press and no creation panel. `core_atome_types.js` declares project intent; no separate project store.
- `dashboard_news_modules.js`: disposable clock/current-weather readings and manual-location panel. Existing Finder geocoder owns debouncing/cancellation; shared Dashboard layout keeps two module cards outside scrolling News, and `items(source, { filteredCategoryId })` omits both while a category filter is open so a filtered display never shows a foreign pinned card. Dashboard shutdown closes the panel and stops its timer/requests.
- `atome_contextual_edit_registry.js` triggers the same deferred contextual owner on first selection; `atome_contextual_edit_runtime.install` consumes canonical selection already present. No second selection listener interprets content or owns state.
- `bevy_text_texture_style.wrapTextLines` owns common word wrapping for labels and text editing; normalization/projection carry explicit wrap width without shrinking fonts.
- Current M/S options are Mute/Solo/All/None. Historical Select All/Delete All descriptions below are superseded.

`ATOME_MIX_PROPERTY_SCHEMA` extends the shared core visual/audio/container schemas with boolean `mute`/`solo` and 0–1 `mute_strength`/`solo_strength` (default 1). `project_view_mix_projection` derives inherited Mute and sibling Solo factors from canonical records. Solo, strengths, audio and painting remain pending.

Interaction owners (2026-09-08): `project_view_molecule_list_model.comparePlaybackRecords` orders persisted hierarchy positions with an immutable-id tie break; List, Matrix and recursive transport share it. `selection.js` updates its canonical runtime selection before publishing. Whole-text colors update existing rich spans via the shared style owner.

`tests/probes/tool_text_record_acceptance.mjs` (MOLECULE_UI_TEXT_TOOLS_ONLY=1).

Molecule/APNG owners (2026-08-29): tool_runtime_molecule_geometry.js reuses the canonical px parser; project_scene_record_projection.js derives nested member bounds without storage migration; render_atom.js and virtual_scene_contract.js enforce transparent structural fill; scene_graph.js excludes envelope hits while surface_interaction_runtime.js preserves member-to-owner gestures. bevy_image_texture_source.js owns bounded source reads, content sniffing and frame-count-aware raster sizing in the existing texture resolver: large Natural bounds keep their logical Bevy size while the precomposed APNG texture stays within the shared 32 MiB decoded-frame budget. animated_png.rs precomposes bounded fitted frames once, skips elapsed infinite-loop cycles and updates the resident Image on its 75 ms clock; animated_png_pixels.rs owns fitted RGBA/alpha preparation. Tests: molecule_visual_geometry.test.mjs, apng_image_resource.test.mjs, tests/rendering/animated_png_tests.rs, tests/probes/apng_image_acceptance.mjs and the dense drag/APNG lane in molecule_drag_performance_acceptance.mjs.
Fitted preview records in `bevy_ui_overlay_record_projection.js` inherit their UI slot's layer and clip, not the source Atome's depth; this keeps image previews above the Visual panel background. The same projection walk is the single clip-state owner for retained panel-scroll records: records outside the viewport keep stable ids but propagate explicit `clippedOut` state and zero opacity through their complete composite subtree, because an empty rectangle intersection and the record contract's “no clip” value are both represented by `null` and must never be conflated.

Urgent interaction campaign (2026-08-28): `project_view_surface_runtime.js`
owns `visual_fullscreen` and projects the existing WebGPU canvas through a
Visualizer-only tree; `project_view_visual_fullscreen_runtime.js` owns its
arm/exit transition without browser fullscreen. `bevy_panel_record_composite_preview.js` resolves the
topmost touched child and `project_view_visual_interaction_runtime.js` remains
the scene-intent/commit owner. The canonical fixed List row accepts handedness;
the contextual rail sorts shared then target-specific tools by stable priority.
`tool_runtime_molecule_geometry.js` removes structural Molecule paint while
retaining canonical bounds and hit-testing. `tool_runtime_line_splitter.js`
creates the sequential text Molecule in one commit batch; recursive transport
derives its Prompter projection from canonical child order.

Natural Molecule interaction stays in the shared scene path:
`surface_pointer_runtime.js` expands an owner recursively,
`surface_text_pointer_runtime.js` feeds owner plus members to the gesture, and
`project_scene_gesture_runtime.js` coalesces in-flight frame commits before its
single final batch. `project_view_mode_state.js` accepts a Natural transition
only when the canonical surface reread confirms the same project and mode.
During saved-project boot, `tool_genesis_project_load_runtime.js` forwards its
already loaded record snapshot through `project_view_mode_state.js` to the
single structured surface owner. `project_view_records.js` normalizes that
snapshot for the first page and `project_view_surface_context_runtime.js` reads
the root playback rule from it, avoiding duplicate native state reads before
`bootPresentationReady` without retaining another cache or state authority.

MIDI ownership is split without duplicating commands: `midi_binding_runtime.js`
owns normalization, matching, conflicts, continuous mapping and ordered Gateway
execution; `midi_binding_service.js` owns persistence, Learn, final-command
capture, Finder tool projection, runtime ports and the shared test monitor/send
contract. `bevy_panel_midi_binding_runtime.js` is the Squirrel/BevyUI mobile
composition. `midi_output_runtime.js` isolates output bytes from binding state;
desktop `native_midi.rs` uses `midir`, while `MIDIController.swift` remains the
iOS/CoreMIDI adapter. Neither owns binding truth.

MIDI capture (2026-10-13) composes the same owners: `rec. > MIDI`
(`eVe/intuition/runtime/eve_intuition/main_menu_capture_content.js`, the rec.
palette entries extracted from `main_menu_content_runtime.js`) runs
`ui.capture.midi` through `intuition/tools/capture.js`; the take lives in
`intuition/tools/midi_capture_runtime.js` (subscribes to the service monitor,
filters the device-local selected input, encodes with `encodeSmfFromEvents` in
`domains/midi/smf.js`, the inverse of `compileSmfEventPlan`) and is stored by
`importMidiMedia` (`intuition/tools/audio_to_midi/result_media.js`), the importer
shared with Audio to MIDI. The long-press input chooser is
`bevy_panel_midi_input_runtime.js` (`ui.midi.input.panel`), assembled from the
canonical select/state/button panel bricks. rec. > Actions left the palette; it
stays on the project rail.

Initial refresh repair (2026-08-27): `ToolRegistryV2.refresh` shares its pending
catalogue read; `tool_registry_storage.js` owns the extracted persistence adapter.
Canonical built-in tools resolve directly from `tool_runtime_bootstrap.js`
definitions and registered handlers, so their first invocation never waits for
the authenticated persistent catalogue or the native Fastify synchronization.
`animation_frame_runtime.js` owns the shared requestAnimationFrame watchdog used
by presentation, panel refresh, image hydration, preview capture, and project
scene scheduling. The reactive Web renderer retains event-driven wakes and a
500 ms idle heartbeat only as a bound for a deferred WKWebView Winit wake;
once no wake has arrived for 3 s the heartbeat stretches to 10 s (settled
workspace, `adapt_web_idle_heartbeat`, 2026-10-09).
Frame clock (2026-10-07): `platforms/web/bevy-renderer/src/frame_clock.rs` owns
the wake path and the heartbeat settings. Every wake — input work and video
frame notifications alike — requests the window's next animation frame once
(`WEB_FRAME_REQUESTED`, re-armed when a tick starts in `web_frame_probe_begin`):
one `RedrawRequested`, one render. A wake merged into an already requested
frame is remembered and the tick that covered it requests the next frame at
`Last` (`continue_merged_wake`), so rAF-driven animation keeps one render per
display frame. Wakes never use a winit `WakeUp` user event once the window
exists: one sent from inside a tick lands after `AboutToWait` and waited for
the 500 ms heartbeat (interaction rendered at 2 frames/s). The drain systems in
`lib.rs` never write `RequestRedraw`: the update that drains work renders it.
`read_atome_bevy_web_diagnostics()` adds the `frame_requests` counter. Real
loop regression probe: `tests/probes/bevy_web_frame_clock_cadence.probe.mjs`.
`bevy_web_renderer_startup.js` is the existing renderer's extracted startup path,
not a second renderer. It installs backing protection before invoking Winit and
refreshes launch geometry after WASM preparation. `surface_runtime.js` projects
the initial workspace reveal requested by `user_workspace_surface_runtime.js`.
`eVe.js` warms the shared WASM module while loading application modules.

UI preparation and motion (2026-08-27): `bevy_ui_mystic_motion.js` derives
child offsets from canonical layout; `bevy_ui_tree_motion_runtime.js` converts
surface-space `overlayPosition` only at the flat renderer boundary. Ordinary
motion uses one renderer batch. Incremental text edits use the same motion API:
the native path emits `update_node_text`, while
`bevy_ui_project_overlay_runtime.js` regenerates only the affected text
resource through `updateProjectSceneRecord`; caret geometry stays on the direct
motion path. `bevy_ui_image_runtime.js` spends preparation
frame budgets on cache misses only and can refresh geometry before publication.
`surface_runtime.js` notifies every effective size change. List/Matrix wait for
their page and contextual geometry before replacing their mounted tree.
`project_workspace_activation_runtime.js` is the extracted lifecycle part of
`project_data.js`; workspace chrome preservation remains in the scene owner.
Every user-facing project open (Matrix, Dashboard card, Finder row) activates
with `staleFirst: true`, so the switch projects the canonical local snapshot and
the load owner schedules its single authoritative refresh after presentation; an
explicit `staleFirst: false` or an empty local snapshot still reaches the
balanced route, which is what keeps an unreachable backend from holding the
project open.
Evidence and remaining acceptance: `known-bug-solutions/ui-preparation-motion/README.md`.

Transport hot path (2026-08-27): `project_view_surface_events.js` refreshes
the contextual rail on recursive state/active-branch transitions, never on
position-only ticks. Existing `patchTransport` owns incremental row/footer
motion. `bevy_video_decode_source_runtime.js` primes a ready image on activation;
subsequent playing ticks do not manufacture decoded-frame notifications.

List filmstrip playback ownership (2026-08-27):
`bevy_panel_record_timeline_preview.js` requests fixed temporal samples from
the shared `recordPreviewNode` with `followPlayback: false`. The shared preview
omits their playback-source association, so `playbackMirrorsFor` does not send
them transport commands. Normal Visualizer/Matrix previews and audio waveforms
retain that association. No second preview component or decoder is added.

Status: Initial framework map after the Atome open / eVe closed boundary validation.

Video seek continuity (2026-08-27): `bevy_video_decode_source_runtime.js`
retains a frame before changing source time and releases it on decoded-frame
arrival or canonical source removal. `bevy_video_stream_source_runtime.js`
owns that bounded frame lifecycle and the existing source lookup, now returning
a presentable video or its retained `VideoFrame`. `video_external_web.rs`
imports either through the same external-texture draw. The Web Cargo target
enables the web-sys 0.3.85 VideoFrame binding; no renderer or DOM surface is added.

Current layered Molecule media ownership (2026-08-25):
`core/media_engine/molecule.js` owns schema-v2 Ensemble transport, atomic
multi-voice start/cleanup, and explicit preview-duration handling;
`molecule_support.js` resolves the existing extracted video-audio asset without
losing its canonical owner; and `molecule_session_clips.js` publishes per-clip
load failures instead of silently degrading the mix. Video pixels remain owned
by `bevy_video_decode_source_runtime.js`, whose retired decoder generations
ignore late load/end/error events, while every audible source (including video
audio) starts exactly once through Kira and the projected decoder stays muted.
`project_view_surface_runtime.js` forces a canonical Natural reconcile before
publishing the mode, retains the projected video source identity, and resets
shared navigation when the project changes. `project_view_records.js` owns that
reset and invalidates pending reads before replacing view state; List, Matrix,
Mix, Timeline, and Atome Detail reuse it. `project_view_insertion_target.js`
rejects a temporal insertion level inherited from another project.

`project_view_molecule_list_model.js` is the shared List/Matrix presentation
resolver: a Molecule expands to its direct `parent_id` Atomes only. Section,
Track and clip records remain exclusive to Timeline and Mixage.
`project_view_list_drag_runtime.js` owns same-level hierarchy reorder and member
extraction on footer Retour; extraction delegates its single batch to
`extractCanonicalMoleculeMember` in `tool_runtime_atome_mutation.js`. A playback reorder persists only `hierarchy_order`. Explicit depth commands and
depth-palette drags persist only visual stack aliases. Both use the same
append-only transaction owner and preserve canonical parent membership.

Repeated Natural absorption still routes through
`surface_interaction_runtime.js` to `absorbCanonicalMolecule` and one
`Atome.commitBatch`: a member hit resolves to the existing Molecule owner, each
new member receives that owner as `parent_id` plus one clip at `t=0`, and new
visual members append above the current maximum `z_index`/`order`. Internal
Plan changes route through the canonical scene intent and alter only persistent
visual depth. `tests/probes/molecule_ui_layered_media.mjs` is the persistent
headed Chromium acceptance owner for the six-member video/audio/text/image
composition, three simultaneous Kira voices, natural timing,
extraction/reabsorption, List hierarchy reorder, cross-mode persistence, and
front/back pixel proof.

Current cross-runtime media-source ownership (2026-08-26):
`domains/media/shared/media_playback_record.js` is the sole record-level owner
for normalized playback kind, protected Web URL, media owner, historical video
audio requirement, and strict-native local path. Molecule transport and
standalone selected-media playback both consume it. The Molecule engine keeps
both URL and path through `core/media_engine/molecule_support.js`, so Tauri Kira
loads the local recording while Web Kira continues to use the canonical API
route. `selected_project_media_playback_state.js` now owns the selected-media
registry, video completion listener, progress timer, publication and teardown;
`selected_project_media_playback_runtime.js` remains the orchestration owner and
no longer duplicates record resolution or playback-state lifecycle.

Current structured playback/reorder ownership (2026-08-22):
`project_view_playback_runtime.js` owns arm and transport lifecycle;
`project_view_playback_item_model.js` owns item classification/default drivers;
`project_view_playback_follow.js` owns armed selection follow. Drag geometry and
the shared 500 ms Natural/List/Matrix threshold lives in
`project_view_drop_intent_runtime.js`; exact ordering, pending transaction
confirmation, and batch persistence live in `project_view_order_runtime.js`.
`project_view_list_drag_runtime.js` and `project_view_matrix_drag_runtime.js`
translate Bevy gestures into those shared intents. The Matrix content and shared
selectable-list preview remain disposable projections; canonical fusion stays in
`tool_runtime_atome_mutation.js`. That public facade reuses
`tool_runtime_atome_mutation_shared.js` for state/commit utilities and
`tool_runtime_atome_duplicate.js` for duplication/remapping.

Current historical-media List guard (2026-08-26):
`backend.kira.js` preserves the positive decoded duration returned by the Web
WASM byte loader. `media_playback_audio_executor.js` carries it through the
shared prepared command, and `selected_project_media_playback_runtime.js`
registers it with `project_audio_playback_progress_runtime.js` before
`project_view_playback_runtime.js` evaluates the current queue item. Imported
videos with absent audio metadata remain audio-required unless the persisted
record explicitly declares no audio; a missing Kira voice therefore fails the
paired start instead of degrading to silent video.
`platforms/web/audio-wasm/src/lib.rs` additionally treats the decoded audio
duration as the hard frame boundary: a longer video-container or Molécule
duration cannot create an out-of-range Kira slice, while a shorter explicit
crop remains intact. The outer transport still follows its longest member and
no audio is stretched.
Natural projection preserves canonical `parent_id` in `render_atom.js`;
`surface_interaction_runtime.js` resolves an absorbed child to its composed
Molecule owner until that owner is explicitly entered, and excludes the dragged
source from overlap hit-testing. A Natural schema-v2 Molecule enters the same
`project_view_molecule_info.js` contextual transport as List and Matrix.

Current native granularity ownership (2026-08-14):
`platforms/desktop-tauri/src/server/local_atome_security.rs` owns local
property ACL decisions and recipient projections;
`local_atome.rs` owns transactional commit/batch enforcement;
`local_atome_extended.rs` owns authenticated sync configuration and projected
history/state commands; `local_atome_sync_worker.rs` owns per-principal
outbound/inbound queue transport and interrupted-queue recovery;
`local_atome_sync_bootstrap.rs` owns deterministic current-state catch-up;
`local_atome_sync_media.rs` owns scoped, idempotent media upload before event delivery; and
`local_atome_remote_projection.rs` owns durable recipient-scoped Fastify projection,
cursors, revocation, remote lifecycle, hierarchy-envelope repair, and preservation
of device-local media references when the device receives its own remote echo.
`atome/src/squirrel/apis/unified/adole_api/auth_fastify_token.js` owns the
WebView-to-native credential handoff and restart reconfiguration. No token or
remote credential is persisted by these owners.

Current surface/teleport ownership (2026-08-15, in progress —
`todo/teleport/`): `server/wsApiState.js` owns the surface registry next to the
connection registry it refines; `server/wsSurfaceOperations.js` owns the `surface`
wire family and its authorization;
`atome/src/squirrel/apis/unified/adole_api/surfaces.js` owns the client-persisted
surface identity, its descriptor, and the announce lifecycle. No second surface
registry may be created: teleport, remote control and destination pickers all consume
these. `atome/src/shared/teleport_state.js` is the sole owner of teleport state
semantics for both boundaries; `server/wsTeleportOperations.js` owns the `teleport`
wire family and pending-offer lifecycle, and reads/commits every object in its owner's
vault (`connection._wsApiVaultRouter` + `_wsApiSyncRuntime.publish`, never the shared
database; hosted objects come from the vault operation `teleport:hosted`);
`atome/src/squirrel/teleport/teleport_manager.js` is the renderer-side owner and only ever
*requests* transitions; `adole_apis.js` imports it so every authenticated surface answers
the offers addressed to it.
`eVe/domains/rendering/teleport_residual_projection.js` owns the residual proxy, applied
inside `normalizeProjectSceneRecords` — the single funnel every record crosses before the
canvas — so an object hosted by another surface becomes a proxy on every path, not only
the teleport tool's (`updateProjectSceneRecord` crosses the same funnel, and a realtime
patch merges onto `describedTeleportRecord`, the description a proxy stands for). The proxy keeps the record id (one object, never a copy), drops the
media source, and uses a fixed badge size that must not be routed through
`normalizeAtomeSizeToMaxAxis` (that helper rescales instead of clamping). Ephemeral scene
records are excluded. The rendering domain never reads the API layer: the manager pushes
surface identity and destination labels through `squirrel:teleport-surface-context`.
`eVe/intuition/tools/teleport.js` owns the four contextual teleport actions and
`hiddenTeleportToolKeysFor`, the sole visibility rule consumed by
`resolveAtomeEditFooterToolDefinitionsForOptions`. Teleport adds no panel and no menu.
That module must keep loading the Teleport Manager through a lazy `import()`: a static
import makes the whole Adole API graph an eager dependency of the contextual footer
(+28 modules per boot), which the mobile performance contract forbids.
`teleport_edge_intent_runtime.js` owns edge-intent detection and its thresholds; its
consumer is the teleport mode `eVe/intuition/tools/teleport_mode.js` (`ui.teleport.mode`,
lit state in `teleport_mode_state.js`, pinned Stop case through `active_tool_registry.js`):
while lit it owns canvas drags through `setProjectSceneDragInterceptor`
(`project_scene_engine.js`) and List row drags through the same slot
(`project_view_list_drag_runtime.js` hands `drag.*` to `sceneState.dragInterceptor` with
the grabbed cell as the dragged rectangle and no `commit`: no landing preview, no
reorder), sends objects pushed past the visible edge with one
`teleport_manager.resolveEdgeIntent(atomeIds, …)` per push, commits every released canvas
object back to its starting place, and Stop returns what it sent (except objects left there). `teleport_destination_picker.js`
owns multi-destination selection and must keep using the contextual editor's
`enterVirtual` rail — teleport may not introduce a device-picker surface of its own.
`server/wsRemoteControlOperations.js`, `atome/src/squirrel/teleport/remote_control_manager.js`
and `eVe/intuition/tools/trackpad.js` own remote control end to end; one-shot remote
commands (2026-10-12) are owned by `server/remoteCommandRelay.js` and
`atome/src/squirrel/teleport/remote_command.js`, on the same family. It is deliberately
independent of teleport and must stay so. The trackpad owns no surface: no element
creation, no panel, no fullscreen — it is a latching toolbox tool that reads pointer
events from the surface already present. `server/surfaceGrants.js` and
`server/wsSurfaceGrantOperations.js` own cross-user authorization through the dedicated
`surface_grants` table; `hasSurfaceCapability` is the only gate teleport and remote
control may call for a cross-account action.
`atome/src/squirrel/teleport/surface_grant_manager.js` is the renderer-side grant owner
and never auto-accepts across accounts; `eVe/intuition/tools/teleport_grant_notifications.js`
feeds the existing inbox rather than adding a second alert surface.
`eVe/intuition/tools/teleport_feedback.js` owns the §20 no-destination signal and the
§11.1 active-session indicator as ephemeral scene records — no modal, no permanent
chrome. `atome/src/squirrel/teleport/remote_preview.js` owns the on-demand still.
`atome/src/squirrel/apis/unified/adole_api/surfaces.js` exports
`ensureRemoteSurfacePrincipal`, which every teleport/remote-control/grant request must
pass: a Tauri or iOS session may be authenticated only against the local backend, and
the relay lives on Fastify. The audit that scoped this work is `todo/teleport/00_audit.md`; it records that
`RemoteCommands`/`BuiltinHandlers` do not exist. Communication no longer imports or
retries that deleted transport: `AdoleAPI.communication.send` uses the authenticated
persist-before-delivery `/ws/api` direct-message owner, while
`adole_websocket_message.js` projects its explicit `eve-comm-share` envelope. The audit also records that
`platforms/desktop-tauri/src/server/remote_control_ws.rs` is loopback-only with six
audio actions — neither is a cross-device transport.

Current property-privacy ownership (2026-08-15): `database/adole_privacy_rules.js` owns
profile output rules and `eVe/intuition/runtime/bevy_panel/bevy_panel_home_privacy.js`
owns their UI, reusing the shared Conditions component rather than adding a second
editor. Rules live in `property_privacy_rules`, are gated inside `canRead`, and are
restrictive only. Granularity validation is delivered:
`todo/audits/granularity_validation_report.md` records verdict `PASS` with a 20-line
conformity matrix and file+line proofs; no corrective change was required.

Current Conditions ownership (2026-08-14): `atome/src/squirrel/conditions/`
owns the sole versioned schema, dynamic property/source registry, safe computed
AST, three-state engine, dependency-aware query lifecycle, static/dynamic lists,
persisted sets/bindings and ADOLE permission adapter.
`atome/src/shared/adole_event_contract.js` owns canonical event-patch extraction and
`server/atomePropertySecurity.js` owns per-property commit authorization and
read projection; `server/conditionsQueryAuthority.js` owns remote discovery and
one-shot query projection. `bevy_panel_conditions.js`,
`bevy_panel_conditions_runtime.js` and projection-only
`bevy_panel_conditions_view.js` are the sole closed eVe Conditions UI;
Finder, Contacts, Communication and Calendar configure that shared component
without local evaluators or DOM authority. Developer contract and current
acceptance boundary: `atome/documentations/conditions.md`.

Current project/media repair ownership (2026-08-11): `server/principal_file_migration.js` owns alias-authorized legacy media reconciliation; `database/adole.js` owns its canonical `file_path` event and the `principal_file_migrations` journal; `server/server_uploads.js` invokes reconciliation only after canonical ACL approval. `project_order_runtime.js` owns deterministic per-user slot reconciliation, while `project_data.js` owns the raw project read, creation, rename, deletion, and project activation. `dashboard_data_controller.js` owns the account-global `projects` cache. `atome_record_utils.js` owns project/parent/owner extraction across root, `properties`, and `meta`. `selected_project_media_playback_runtime.js` owns aggregate playback state and audio lifecycle; `media_reader_tool_runtime.js` alone bridges video decoder transport. Mystic and the Atome footer are projections of that state.

Current workspace-entry contract (2026-07-22; supersedes older statements that make Dashboard opening wait for project readiness): `user_workspace_surface_runtime.js` owns one Dashboard-first orchestration. It mounts and verifies the neutral Dashboard and main menu first, then invokes the existing `project_bootstrap.js` readiness owner behind that foreground and keeps the Dashboard visible after loading completes. The prepared project is revealed only when the user explicitly closes the Dashboard through `toggleWorkspaceDashboardAndMainMenu`. A project bootstrap failure leaves the mounted Dashboard intact and returns an explicit `project_bootstrap` phase; Dashboard mount failures return `dashboard_open`, while an explicit project-reveal failure returns `project_reveal`. This flow is shared by authenticated, anonymous, and `boot_runtime.js` entry; it never creates a second renderer or changes media/recording paths.

Current assistant ownership (2026-08-10): `eVe/voice/assistant/` owns the closed full-screen Bevy assistant and the Atome-tool 520 ms toggle; `atome/src/squirrel/voice/assistant_session_controller.js` owns conversational turns, shared Home endpoint thresholds, a half-duplex TTS/acoustic-drain guard, residual anti-echo filtering, and strict MCP execution selection. Native STT is never opened while local TTS is active. `orchestrator_bridge.js` owns MCP transport, while `orchestrator_agent_toolchain.js` owns MCP AI toolchain result/confirmation normalization. `provider_client.js` owns active-provider/key resolution. Home's Bevy provider/model/key UI remains in `bevy_panel_home_{view,vault_runtime}.js`; it persists metadata in `profile.passkeys.keys` and secrets only in the token vault.

Current mobile performance contract (2026-07-17; supersedes older warmup/preview-density details below wherever they conflict):

- `eVe/intuition/runtime/eve_intuition/boot_runtime.js` performs no workspace/tool/panel/voice warmup before presentation. Existing workspace modules may warm after `bootPresentationReady`; voice/ONNX waits a further bounded delay and capture/delete stay lazy through `panel_definitions.js`. Camera/microphone permission is requested only by an explicit capture action. `eVe/eVe.js` does not speculatively instantiate renderer WASM.
- `surface_size_runtime.js` caps the shared render surface at DPR 1.5. Dashboard detail textures use scale 2, card media uses scale 1..1.5, and `bevy_media_texture_cache.js` owns a byte-weighted 16 MiB / 96-entry LRU. `bevy_ui_runtime.js` receives pointer/wheel input only through the canonical surface interceptor.
- `surface_size_runtime.js` also owns iOS keyboard viewport arbitration, reusing
  `atome/src/utils/ios_runtime.js#isIOSDevice`. A same-width contracted visual
  viewport wins over stale fullscreen host/window/native dimensions on every
  measure, so text creation cannot transiently expand the shared canvas.
- `AdoleAPI.projects.list()` excludes `preview_url` by default all the way through Tauri SQLite, iOS SQLite, and Fastify/PostgreSQL query projection. `project_security.js` accepts canonical `meta.owner_id`; list errors remain explicit. Missing thumbnails never launch bulk captures. Explicit active-project preview capture uses one ephemeral iframe, DPR 1 WebP quality 0.72, and destroys the capture renderer in `finally`.
- `dashboard_bevy_ui_runtime.js` coalesces rendering to one in-flight projection plus one latest-state slot and hydrates only viewport-visible lanes, loading newly revealed lanes on scroll. `bevy_video_decode_source_runtime.js` pauses hidden videos and cancels RAF/RVFC while Dashboard owns the workspace, except playback requests flagged `presentation` (a UI preview on the Dashboard). Browser Bevy Winit uses explicit wakes only, with no idle update cadence.
- `user_login_visual_contract.js` supplies bounded logo entrance timing; `user_login_logo_runtime.js` consumes the canonical frame scheduler and shared UI-tree motion. The previous gradient/mask/painter and credential DOM routes are removed.
- `platforms/ios/atome-auv3/Common/FileSyncCoordinator.swift` owns event-driven local/AppGroup/iCloud propagation. Initialization and concrete mutation owners call `syncAll()` explicitly; the former repeating auto-sync timer is deleted. `LocalHTTPServer.swift` serves GET resources without launching a whole-root synchronization pass.

Current account/guest ownership (2026-09-27): `server/wsPhoneLinkAuth.js` owns device-proof authentication; the former public provision action is rejected; `server/wsApiGuestAdoption.js` and `server/wsApiGuestAdoptionFiles.js` own the remote guest-adoption journal and staged file completion. `server/wsApiIdentity.js` verifies that a JWT principal is a persisted Fastify user before remote events, state, snapshots, sync, sharing, or direct messages proceed. Browser guest identity is owned by `adole_api/session.js`, `auth_methods_session_account.js`, and IndexedDB-only `guest_workspace_store.js`; it is CSPRNG UUID v4 local state, not a user Atome or remote credential. `auth_workspace.js` keeps adoption explicit and sends the confirmed browser manifest through `/ws/api` before it clears the local guest space.

Current production identity deployment contract (2026-08-10): `scripts/server_secure_config.js` is the single deployment owner for JWT/cookie secrets and the persistent Fastify signing identity used by inter-runtime account provisioning. It validates configured RSA key pairs, fails closed on incomplete or mismatched paths, generates one stable identity under `/etc/squirrel/identity` when absent, records absolute key paths plus a stable server id/name in `/etc/squirrel/squirrel.env`, enforces key permissions, and backs the identity up before an update. `scripts/server_update.js` only orchestrates this owner; `server/serverIdentity.js` owns key creation, validation, and fingerprint derivation at runtime and for the manual generator.

Purpose:

- Provide the mandatory first navigation layer before future implementation work.
- Identify ownership, reusable modules, entry points, duplication risks, and areas that require targeted verification.
- Avoid using `docs/` as a root map directory. Framework maps live under `maps/`; product and framework documentation remain in `eve/documentations/` and `atome/documentations/`.

Mandatory Use:

- Before adding or changing code, consult this file first, then perform targeted code verification.
- Reuse, extend, or factorize existing modules before creating a new one.
- Update this map when ownership, structure, reusable APIs, or duplication risks change.

## Mandatory Pre-Implementation Gate

No implementation, refactor, cleanup, API work, UI work, tool work, persistence work, security work, or runtime behavior change may start until the relevant maps have been consulted:

- Start with this file to identify source ownership, existing modules, reusable boundaries, entry points, and known duplication or size risks.
- Consult `maps/API_MAP.md` before touching APIs, runtime globals, server routes, MCP surfaces, tools, persistence contracts, or exported methods.
- Consult `maps/DESIGN_MAP.md` before touching tokens, JavaScript-generated styles, visual factories, panels, tools, Matrix, Molecule/MTraX visuals, or assets.
- Consult `maps/ARCHITECTURE_MAP.md` before touching dependency direction, lifecycle, command/history flow, sync, server/database boundaries, cross-layer ownership, or runtime modes.

Implementation may proceed only after the map check identifies the owning layer, confirms whether reusable code already exists, and determines which maps must be updated in the same task.

### Canonical component owners — 2026-08-07, authoritative

These responsibilities have exactly one implementation. Consuming one is always
allowed; writing a second is never allowed. When an owner cannot serve a need,
add a parameter to it, defaulting to the existing behaviour.

| Responsibility | Single owner |
| --- | --- |
| Inline text editing (session, caret, selection, commit) | `eVe/intuition/runtime/bevy_panel/bevy_panel_text_editing.js` |
| Text field node (paint, focus, alignment) | `eVe/intuition/runtime/bevy_panel/bevy_panel_editable_text.js` |
| Text geometry, word and line ranges | `eVe/domains/rendering/text_editing_layout.js` |
| Hidden text / IME service | `eVe/domains/rendering/hidden_text_service_runtime.js` |
| List row, hierarchy, selection, drag | `eVe/intuition/runtime/bevy_panel/bevy_panel_selectable_list.js` |
| Virtualized scrolling window | `eVe/intuition/runtime/bevy_panel/bevy_panel_virtual_window.js` |
| Table column geometry | `atome/src/squirrel/components/table_contract.js` |
| Sortable column header | `eVe/intuition/runtime/bevy_panel/bevy_panel_sortable_header.js` |
| Media card and tile | `eVe/intuition/runtime/bevy_panel/bevy_panel_media_card.js` |
| Panel shell, footer, scroll area | `eVe/intuition/runtime/bevy_panel/bevy_panel_tree.js` |
| Empty / loading / error state | `eVe/intuition/runtime/bevy_panel/bevy_panel_state.js` |
| Visual tokens | `EVE_PANEL_SKIN_TOKENS` in `eVe/elements/skin/panel_skin.js` |
| Project name, deletion, duplication and project clipboard | `eVe/intuition/matrix/core/project_data.js` (`updateProjectName`, `deleteProjectRecord`, `duplicateProjectRecord`, `copyProjectSnapshot`, `pasteProjectSnapshot`) |

Enforced by `npm run check:component-reuse-guardrails`, wired into `check:m0`.
Added after an audit found the inline text editor in six copies — 981 lines over
a 750-line canonical stack — and the virtualized window in three, despite the
reuse rule already being in force. Full rationale and the operative clause are
in `.codex/modules/04-feature-work-cleanup-and-framework-reuse.md`, section
*CANONICAL COMPONENT OWNERS*.

## Global Ownership

BevyUI panel ownership:

- `todo/ui_bevy/bevy_panel_migration_guide.md` is the Priority 1 execution
  ledger for five migration packages, shared panel-style token groups,
  component-level contracts, package-level browser verification and
  product-owner approval, product-panel coverage, and final HTML-route
  retirement gates.
- `eVe/intuition/runtime/bevy_panel/` owns the closed eVe BevyUI panel shell: tokens, mobile/desktop geometry, pure tree construction, surface registration, and BevyUI mount/unmount lifecycle over `window.eveBevyUiRuntime`.
- `eVe/intuition/runtime/bevy_panel/bevy_panel_runtime.js` must use the installed canonical BevyUI canvas runtime (never create a competing pointer owner), own ephemeral panel geometry plus Panel Lab's opt-in footer fullscreen/restoration state, and reassert the authenticated/workspace main-menu invariant after mounting a BevyUI panel surface, using the shared workspace menu helper instead of panel-local HTML chrome.
- `eVe/intuition/runtime/eve_intuition/atome_contextual_edit_model.js` owns the disposable Atome-edit footer tree: an exact-bounds transparent composed shell alone carries the shared exterior shadow, while the sibling selection outline remains shadow-free and cannot expand that silhouette.
- `eVe/intuition/ribbon/bevy_ui_menu_surface.js` owns the shared Bevy footer geometry, common menu-tool content/node builders, plus the corner-resize grip and red-close constructors. Panel footer trees retain the shared grips. Atome-edit footers use the shared title/close surface without object resize grips.
- `eVe/intuition/shared/bevy_ui_tool_slider.js` owns the shared canvas-only
  vertical tool-slider presentation and delegates its pure ephemeral session
  transitions to the canonical Atome/Squirrel slider owner.
  It composes the existing BevyUI menu material, label, image, and panel
  primitives because the Rust `tool_slider` kind has no dedicated native rail,
  thumb, value, or vertical-slider widget. Contextual editing keeps canonical
  tool invocation ownership. The code is retained for future tool-slider
  design review, not as a Panel Lab or generic panel component.
  `bevy_ui_pointer_runtime.js` exempts `tool_slider` and every node with an
  explicit `drag` handler from panel-scroll drag takeover, so its vertical
  gesture remains owned by the descendant interaction.
- `eVe/intuition/runtime/bevy_panel/bevy_panel_accordion.js` owns the validated
  shared stateless panel accordion tree: native interactive header, localized-
  label slot, filled disclosure image, and optional body-child slot composed only from
  `EVE_PANEL_SKIN_TOKENS.bevyPanel`.
- `atome/src/squirrel/components/select_contract.js` owns renderer-neutral
  Select option and value normalization. `bevy_panel_select.js` composes that
  canonical contract into the available native BevyUI interactive primitives:
  a `select` header and generic `button` option rows. Product selection state belongs to each consuming surface. `bevy_ui_hit_test_runtime.js` traverses an
  overflow-visible descendant outside its non-clipping parent while preserving
  hidden/scroll clip boundaries, and `bevy_ui_scroll_runtime.js` includes that
  visible descendant in a scroll area's reveal extent. Those shared owners make
  the Select popup interactive and revealable without reserving panel body flow.
- `atome/src/squirrel/components/toggleable_contract.js` owns renderer-neutral
  normalization for the independent two-state controls (`checkbox`, `switch`).
  A radio group has the option/value semantics of a Select, so `radioGroupNode`
  reuses `select_contract.js` instead of a second option contract.
  `bevy_panel_choice.js` composes both into the native `checkbox`, `radio`, and
  `toggle` kinds around `buildBevyIconButtonSurfaceNode`, the same canonical
  30 px surface used by icon actions. The shared
  Select check mark now lives once in `bevy_panel_tree.js` as `checkMarkNode`
  and serves both Select and the checkbox.
- `atome/src/squirrel/components/table_contract.js` owns renderer-neutral table
  column/row normalization and the fluid column-width resolution: fixed `widthPx`
  columns are subtracted first, the remainder is split by `flex`, and the last
  fluid column absorbs the rounding remainder so columns always sum exactly to
  the table width. `bevy_panel_table.js` composes that contract into the passive
  native `table` root, `row` rows, `text` cells, and `divider` rules; it holds no
  state, handler, or measurement, and the DOM `table_builder.js` route is
  rejected. `bevy_panel_runtime.js` remains the single geometry authority and
  passes `bodyWidth` into `buildContent`, so a fluid component never measures the
  DOM. `panelBodyLayer` in `bevy_panel_tree.js` resolves a node's `z_index`
  against the layer its parent received, so an elevated subtree such as the
  Select popup keeps its whole content above later body siblings.
- `bevy_ui_scroll_runtime.js` owns bounded, inertia-cancelling post-rebuild
  target reveal inside existing scroll areas. `bevy_ui_runtime.js` exposes it
  only through the closed `revealTreeNode({ id, nodeId, marginPx })` runtime
  bridge; panel runtime invokes that bridge after a surface intent requests a
  reveal, never through a second scroll owner. `sourceTrees` retains the
  hydrated unscrolled input; every scroll frame derives the overlay and hit-test
  trees from that source without replacing it with a clipped viewport artifact.
- `eVe/intuition/runtime/eve_intuition/panel_surface_runtime.js` remains the existing `openPanelSurface` / `closePanelSurface` bridge. It invokes a declared session-aware window owner after its lazy module load, and routes other registered surfaces to the BevyUI panel runtime.
- `eVe/intuition/tools/timeline.js` is now a compatibility wrapper only; the visible Timeline product-tool surface is `timelineSurface` in `bevy_panel_surfaces.js` and must not recreate `createEveDialog`, tool docks, or visible HTML controls. Timeline is the sixteenth and final product-panel migration; its existing Bevy route is not completion evidence and its controls must not be counted as generic Panel components.
- Calendar is lazy-registered by `eVe/intuition/tools/calendar.js`; `bevy_panel_calendar_runtime.js` owns its disposable editor mode, quick-create draft, and gesture state, while `bevy_panel_calendar_projection.js` owns time-slot snapping and multi-week month segments. Month/week/day double-clicks and draft/persisted drag-resize gestures remain BevyUI intentions; only validated saves and persisted-event gesture releases reach the existing Calendar service. The common registry carries no Calendar stub.
- `bevy_panel_tree.js` owns shared panel text, divider, composition-row, and passive-list-row builders above the opaque shell. `textNode` consumes canonical panel typography and `dividerNode` consumes canonical divider paint/margins. `listRowNode` configures the native BevyUI `row` primitive with existing panel control geometry, system surface, and localized text; it never adds an interaction handler, state owner, or DOM projection. `EVE_BUTTON_SKIN_TOKENS.bevyButton` is the single standard BevyUI button-style owner: common material/depth, radius, focus ring, pressed translation, disabled opacity, semantic tones, and icon-action geometry. `buildBevyIconButtonNode`, labeled panel action buttons, and standard/palette menu tools consume that contract. Mystic reuses the material but explicitly overrides the standard radius with its preference-controlled tile roundness; chips, segmented groups, close rings, and other composite controls retain only their required shape exceptions. Role-local rest/pressed/active shadow maps are removed. `bevy_ui_tree_normalization.js`, `bevy_ui_overlay_record_projection.js`, and `bevy_media_texture_resolver.js` carry the row label's normalized vertical alignment and +1 px offset to the text texture. The panel row places the localized label as a sibling on the right, never inside the button. `bevy_panel_surfaces.js` remains only the product-surface registration owner.
- `atome/src/squirrel/components/action_button_contract.js` owns the renderer-neutral labeled action-button presentation contract: localized label, `neutral`/`destructive` variant, and busy/disabled handler suppression. `bevy_panel_tree.js` consumes it through the shared native `button` builder, while `bevy_panel_lab_action_button_runtime.js` owns only the Lab's reset-on-close hover/focus/press snapshot and closed `panel_lab.action_button.*` intents. The validated state matrix has no DOM control, MCP call, durable state, or Atome mutation.
- `atome/src/squirrel/components/segmented_control_contract.js` owns the validated renderer-neutral segmented-control normalization: two or more unique options and one known selected value. `bevy_panel_segmented_control.js` composes it from the supported native `segmented_control` root and native button primitives because the active WASM vocabulary has no specialized segmented-widget renderer. `bevy_panel_lab_segmented_control_runtime.js` owns only reset-on-close `list`/`table`/`natural` presentation state and closed `panel_lab.segmented.*` intents; canonical foreground-scene records and hit-tests cover all three segments. It creates no DOM control, MCP call, durable state, or Atome mutation.
- `atome/src/squirrel/components/selectable_list_contract.js` owns the in-review renderer-neutral exclusive-list contract: two or more unique options and one required enabled selected value. `bevy_panel_selectable_list.js` composes the supported native `button` primitive because native `row` is non-interactive; it reuses Select presentation states and the shared check mark. `bevy_panel_lab_selectable_list_runtime.js` owns only reset-on-close `first`/`second` Lab presentation state and closed `panel_lab.selectable_list.*` intents. It creates no DOM control, MCP call, durable state, or Atome mutation.
- `atome/src/squirrel/components/multiline_input_contract.js` owns renderer-neutral newline-preserving local draft presentation. `bevy_panel_editable_text.js` supplies the shared editable tree, while `bevy_panel_multiline_input.js` composes the `358 × 80 px` panel field and `text_editing_layout.js` provides its opt-in wrapped WebGPU lines. `bevy_panel_lab_multiline_input_runtime.js` owns only close-reset draft, caret, and scroll presentation state and emits no MCP call, mutation, or DOM control.
- `atome/src/squirrel/components/scope_chip_contract.js` owns renderer-neutral multi-value filter presentation. `bevy_panel_scope_chips.js` composes native buttons with Select-derived state paint; `bevy_panel_lab_scope_chip_runtime.js` owns only close-reset `events`/`todos`/`reminders` selection and closed `panel_lab.scope_chip.*` intents. It creates no public API, durable state, MCP call, or Atome mutation.
- `atome/src/squirrel/components/panel_state_contract.js` owns the renderer-neutral passive status presentation: `empty`, `loading`, `error`, or `permission_denied`, each with required localized title and message. `bevy_panel_state.js` composes the native non-interactive `empty_state` kind from the shared panel state skin; `bevy_panel_lab_surface.js` renders all four specimens statically. It has no handler, Lab state, MCP call, Atome mutation, or DOM control.
- `atome/src/squirrel/components/numeric_input_contract.js` owns renderer-neutral numeric-field presentation: finite caller-owned value/range/step, localized unit, and disabled state. `bevy_panel_numeric_field.js` composes the existing native `button` and `number_input` primitives; `bevy_panel_lab_numeric_field_runtime.js` owns only close-reset direct-edit, stepper, focus, and scrub-drag presentation state with closed `panel_lab.numeric_field.*` intents. It creates no MCP call, Atome mutation, public API, durable state, or DOM control.
- `bevy_panel_numeric_field_runtime.js` is the shared numeric-field interaction owner for direct editing, standard step buttons, focus, and scrub phases; Panel Lab and the Size product surface configure that one owner. `bevy_panel_size_runtime.js` composes it with standard scope chips and the shared selection summary, while `bevy_panel_font_runtime.js` composes the standard selectable list. Their small `tools/size.js` and `tools/font.js` bridges retain the existing public panel/apply ids and register only shared-canvas Bevy surfaces. Panel intentions re-enter those public ids through `invokeToolGateway`; business writes remain in `selection_style_apply.js`.
- `atome/src/squirrel/components/media_card_contract.js` owns passive media-card normalization for a caller-owned `ready`/`loading`/`error` status, localized title/message, media source when ready, and accessibility label. `bevy_panel_media_card.js` uses the existing shared `image` hydration route and the validated panel-state builder; it owns neither image loading nor a handler. `atome/src/squirrel/components/selection_summary_contract.js` owns a localized title/summary plus nonnegative count; `bevy_panel_selection_summary.js` is its passive canvas projection. These Family 9/17 technical prototypes have no MCP call, mutation, DOM control, or Lab state, but are superseded as active Panel-migration evidence: they are not counted or reused until their actual product owner, legacy occurrence, and measured geometry are established.
- `bevy_panel_lab_text_input_runtime.js` owns only the Panel Lab field policy:
  consume the first enabled activation without mutating the value, preserve all
  user-entered values, validate Return as blur, and reset on panel
  close/reload. `bevy_panel_tree.js` hides the placeholder only while the empty
  field is focused. `text_editing_session.js`
  owns the shared submit/IME arbitration. `surface_runtime.js` and
  `surface_text_pointer_runtime.js` route a double-click on active project text
  to `text.selection.all`; `project_scene_text_runtime.js` delegates that
  selection to `text_bridge.js` without reopening or committing the session.
- `text_editing_session.js` also owns focused-editor document
  `selectionchange` synchronization, and it applies the shared touch-target rule
  of `hidden_text_service_runtime.js` (`setActiveTextEditorHitTest`): focus
  returns the target, a retained blur that does not reclaim the focus withdraws
  it, so a hidden editor kept mounted for scene text can no longer open the iOS
  keyboard on the first tool tap that reaches its band. Native selection-only movement, including
  the iOS keyboard trackpad, updates the disposable selection/caret snapshot
  and reuses the existing WebGPU text projection without creating input or a
  second editor.
- `tests/eve/bevy_panel_contract.test.mjs` guards the shared Bevy panel-shell contract: no tools dock or passive header, body is the single scroll area, and the footer owns title/close/drag/resize. Timeline's independent product-tool surface no longer builds an HTML dialog.
- `tests/eve/bevy_ui_layout_contract.test.mjs` owns the focused parity contract between BevyUI overlay layout and canvas hit testing for flex padding, gaps, native dividers, and control margins.

Dashboard Bevy ownership:

- `eVe/default_values/constants.json` owns the seed data for dashboard categories. The default categories are data, not duplicated source constants.
- `eVe/domains/dashboard/dashboard_preferences.js` owns only the `preferences.dashboard.weather_location` normalization; the Dashboard category filter of Home was removed on 2026-09-22.
- `eVe/domains/dashboard/` owns the closed eVe dashboard runtime: JSON default loading, category/item normalization, pure layout, visual tokens, Bevy UI tree projection, data adapters, environment resize/handedness observation, and main Atom handle toggle lifecycle.
- `eVe/domains/dashboard/dashboard_environment.js` owns dashboard environment reads and shared key normalization: current project resolution, registered BevyUI main-menu reserved height, handedness, compact environment snapshots/signatures, and the filtered-category item projection (the cascade that redistributes a filtered category from its own lane across the other lanes). It has no legacy toolbox DOM discovery or measurement fallback and owns no durable project state or DOM rendering.
- `eVe/domains/dashboard/dashboard_environment_watcher.js` owns the dashboard-only live environment observer while the dashboard is open. It watches the project surface, surface parent, toolbox candidates, window resize, Intuition handedness state, and profile preference updates, then coalesces environment rerenders only when the environment signature changes. Its last renderable snapshot is reused by `dashboard_bevy_ui_runtime.js` for render layout so pointer/wheel/fade frames do not repeat toolbox measurement and transient zero/invalid resize snapshots cannot collapse a visible Dashboard. Category-visibility preference changes are handled by `dashboard_bevy_ui_runtime.js` through the same profile-preference event so category lists are recomputed before render.
- `eVe/domains/dashboard/dashboard_bevy_ui_runtime.js` owns the dashboard BevyUI lifecycle, data hydration, render scheduling, action routing, label-edit composition, and neutral-workspace open/close flow. `dashboard_bevy_ui_tree.js` owns the pure Dashboard record-to-BevyUI-tree projection, while `dashboard_bevy_ui_scroll_runtime.js` owns runtime-only wheel and pointer-drag overflow offsets, axis lock, snap animation, and activation suppression after scroll. These modules remain internal dashboard projection/interaction owners and must not become a second project renderer or durable scene owner.
- `eVe/domains/dashboard/dashboard_open_state.js` owns the small dashboard-open helpers shared by the runtime: critical first-paint category selection, profile-dashboard preference reads, the 180 ms opening input guard constant, and dynamic dashboard layer offset computation from the project scene stack.
- `eVe/domains/dashboard/dashboard_actions.js` owns dashboard item click actions and reasserts the workspace main menu after project-card activation. A short press on the lower third of a header toggles the Dashboard creation mode (`dashboard_creation_runtime.js`) while a short press on its upper two thirds toggles the category filter (`dashboard_bevy_ui_runtime.js#toggleCategoryFilter`), the two modes being exclusive (`dashboard_creation_runtime.js`); calendar/contact cells only open the registered editor through `openPanelSurface` and never load the Contacts API or mutate the directory. Entêtes still expose no `+` creation strip or plus rail. In filtered category mode, item hit-testing keeps the real item category separate from the visual lane category that received the cascade. Project item clicks enter workspace `transition`, fade out and tear down the dashboard, then activate/load that project as the active Bevy desktop instead of opening a dashboard fullscreen detail. The Dashboard rail shows the visible category headers only: the former Settings header, which opened Home through that surface session owner [ensureToolModule(home) then window.open_home_panel] rather than calling openPanelSurface(home) directly, is removed, so the main-toolbar Home tool is the single entry point of the Home panel and the Dashboard never opens it.
- `eVe/domains/rendering/surface_runtime.js` exposes the product-neutral `setRenderSurfaceInteractionInterceptor()` hook plus priority layers through `setRenderSurfaceInteractionInterceptorLayer()` so canvas-owned overlays can intercept pointer/wheel input before project drag, resize, selection, or text edit routing. `surface_interaction_runtime.js` owns the corresponding project-canvas listener lifecycle and routes one authoritative Pointer Event sequence for mouse, touch, or pen; a pointer acquired by BevyUI remains owned through move, release/cancel, and a 900 ms/12 px bounded derived-click guard, so iOS keyboard presentation cannot redirect the terminal event into project selection or native blur. Compatibility `mousedown`/`mouseup` and raw touch listeners are not parallel activation paths. Native `click`/`dblclick` remain semantic terminal events only and never emit BevyUI `activate`. `intuition/runtime/input_event_runtime.js` applies the same Pointer Event-only lifecycle to registered DOM gestures and detaches its shared document listeners after the last registration. The hooks and gesture sessions are runtime-only and store no business state in DOM.
- `eVe/domains/rendering/workspace_scene_layers.js` owns the closed eVe workspace scene layer contract above the shared project scene: `workspace_root`, `project_layer`, `dashboard_layer`, `panel_layer`, `main_menu_layer`, and `mystic_layer`. Project records, Dashboard records, and BevyUI trees pass through this normalizer or its decorator so each projected record has a logical `parent_id`, normalized `properties.layer`, derived render layer, accessible action metadata, and the explicit inherited `presentation` flag when the complete tree must stay outside workspace backdrop capture. `bevy_ui_tree_normalization.js` preserves and propagates that tree flag through the runtime normalization boundary. Presentation is tree metadata, never inferred from a product tree id. The module also owns internal workspace scene diagnostics and accessibility-graph derivation; it is not a second renderer, public API, DOM state store, or canvas owner.
- Dashboard visuals are projected as disposable logical-coordinate records into the existing project Bevy surface through `project_scene_runtime.js`; there is no dashboard DOM renderer, no per-item DOM node, no dashboard-side scale transform, and no second visible canvas. Empty categories do not emit placeholder cards. `dashboard_workspace_mode.js` owns the workspace-mode contract and the neutral Dashboard scene id `__eve_dashboard_workspace__`; its host `project_view___eve_dashboard_workspace__` is a fixed full-viewport shell before the first projection so Dashboard layout never derives from the canvas default content height. Default boot renders the Dashboard into that neutral scene and must not load a user project, even in the background. A user project is loaded only after a Dashboard project-card starts the `transition` mode, fades out the Dashboard, removes its records, and then calls the shared project activation path.
