Default category order is Calendar, News, Contacts, Monitor, Projects from top to bottom. `dashboard_tokens.js` owns the fixed block sizing contract: `resolveDashboardBlockUnitSize()` derives the Dashboard unit from `TOOL_UI_METRICS.toolboxSquareSizePx * metrics.blockUnitMultiplier` (default multiplier `2`), so lane height, card unit width, and entête width stay tied to the canonical main-toolbox tool size instead of stretching to the viewport. `dashboard_layout.js` owns the bottom-aligned vertical viewport above `toolbox_reserved_rect`; Projects is flush with its lower edge when all lanes fit, all spare space remains above the stack, normal resting snaps use whole lane steps, and the terminal snap uses the real scroll max. Partially intersecting lanes keep their complete lane/header/card rectangles at the raw scrolled position and expose separate visible intersections; `dashboard_record_primitives.js` applies the resulting disposable `properties.clip` metadata to every related shape, image, and text record, and hit-testing reads only the visible intersection. It also keeps lane body rectangles stretched across the full available WebView width up to the entête column, exposes each lane's HTML-overflow-style `horizontal_scroll_max`, exposes the horizontal snap anchor on the real entête side (`right` in right-handed layout, `left` in left-handed layout), clamps rendered lane offsets to that range, accepts continuous per-lane offsets through `allowPartialItems`, and allows the opposite WebView edge to show a partial card while the entête-side card remains whole at rest. Focused mode does not reserve a plus rail; former plus geometry is normal lane surface. `dashboard_bevy_ui_scroll_runtime.js` normalizes runtime offsets from the resolved layout, clamps visible lanes, preserves still-valid offscreen lane offsets, and removes offsets for categories no longer present. The open dashboard uses one low-layer project veil record and one vertical `bottom_shadow` texture-gradient record; it must not project a mask across the toolbox-reserved band because the foreground menu must remain visually unchanged. Dashboard Bevy records are decorative/runtime records with `selectable: false`; `dashboard_bevy_ui_runtime.js` offsets the Dashboard layer band above any current durable project stack only when rendering over a project scene, reconciles only the currently visible Dashboard records, and on close removes every `__eve_dashboard_` record from the scene instead of hiding or parking it. Dashboard open/close fade is owned by `dashboard_projection_lifecycle.js` and mutates only Dashboard Bevy record opacity for the canonical `dashboardFadeMs` interval; canvas opacity and neutral host opacity are not part of the visual effect. Warmup is data-only: it loads category/item cache without building Dashboard records or touching the Bevy scene, and its texture phase derives closed-scene warmable records for reachable horizontal snap layouts so offscreen card text keys can be cached without spawning Dashboard records. `project_scene_stack_runtime.js` excludes Dashboard/Molecule ephemeral records from durable media/drop stack allocation while clamping new durable records below visible Dashboard records. Dashboard close/refresh/project-change cleanup reconciles all known `__eve_dashboard_` ids from runtime state and Bevy virtual scenes so late RAF renders or orphan scene entries cannot stay visible. Dashboard interaction is owned by `dashboard_bevy_ui_runtime.js`: pointer and wheel gestures keep smooth continuous horizontal/vertical offsets during movement, ignore horizontal scroll when a lane has no overflow, use physical overflow-style horizontal direction independent of handedness (`deltaX > 0` increases the offset and dragging right decreases it), clamp every horizontal mutation and inertial target to `horizontal_scroll_max`, use axis lock for pointer drags, then inertially animate to the nearest valid card/lane snap point anchored to the entête side at rest; the Dashboard BevyUI root hit-test area stops at `dashboard_rect.height` so the toolbox-reserved band remains owned visually and interactively by the canonical BevyUI main menu. Fresh opens clear any focused category, invalidate in-flight warmup through `warmupSerial`, and arm a 180 ms opening input guard so only the pointer sequence that opened the dashboard is swallowed. Warmup aborts while the runtime is active, opening, closing, or serial-invalidated, so data-only preloads cannot overwrite a visible open layout. The dormant `backdrop_blur` effect path is kept for later renderer work but is not emitted by default.
- `eVe/domains/dashboard/dashboard_records.js` owns image-backed Dashboard card projection and stable per-item Dashboard record ids (`card_<category>_<item>` plus typed child ids) for card shape, media, backdrop, and text records. `eVe/domains/dashboard/dashboard_record_primitives.js` owns the small record/text/image/rect helpers plus opacity application so record creation stays shared and allocation-light; it marks the skin's already-absolute Dashboard layers before workspace normalization, keeping those records in the Dashboard band and below the Mystic presentation band. `eVe/domains/dashboard/dashboard_svg_sources.js` owns the static SVG data-url sources used by Dashboard icons, header side-shadow gradients, bottom shadow gradients, and card label backdrops; it is a projection asset helper, not a renderer or state owner. Card shape fill is a slight `+3%` same-hue lift from the lane entête color so cards stay visually readable over the lane body while preserving the rubrique color family. Contact cards may emit one Bevy image record from normalized `metadata.user_face` between the card shape and Bevy text, using `media_fit: "cover"` plus the card content `corner_radius`. Project cards may emit one Bevy image record from renderer-owned `metadata.project_preview_source`, using `media_fit: "contain"` plus the same card content `corner_radius` and propagated natural dimensions so the full project miniature stays visible, keeps its source ratio, and lets the existing card color show through margins. Header SVG icons and detailed text labels keep the shared high-density `texture_scale: 4` contract, while card media uses `max(2, devicePixelRatio)` through `dashboardCardMediaTextureScale()` so project/contact thumbnails avoid unnecessary 4x raster uploads; both routes are consumed by the common Bevy media texture resolver and are not Dashboard-only rasterizers. Dashboard image and text texture hydration is budgeted by `eVe/domains/rendering/bevy_media_resource_runtime.js`: structural shape records spawn immediately with transparent pending textures for Dashboard image/text records, then the shared deferred queue resolves up to three textures per frame and discards any async result whose stable-id content no longer matches the current Virtual Scene, ordered card media first, label backdrops second, card text/date third, headers after that, and non-dashboard media last. Image-backed cards also emit one bottom-only-rounded `card_label_backdrop_*` Bevy image record between the media and text; it spans the full card content width, uses a bounded label band with a `24px` logical minimum and `42px` cap, uses the same card fill at `0.6` opacity, and the matching title text record is constrained to that exact band with middle baseline and no vertical padding. `eVe/domains/rendering/render_atom.js` carries `media_fit` / `object_fit` and optional `texture_scale` into disposable media content, while `eVe/domains/rendering/bevy_media_texture_image_fit.js` and `bevy_media_texture_resolver.js` own the product-neutral contain/cover draw rect, per-node texture density, cache key separation, and rounded alpha clipping before the texture reaches Bevy. `eVe/domains/rendering/project_preview_runtime.js` resolves persisted preview metadata, invalidates its derived cache on every forced capture, excludes the workspace surface descriptor, captures from current project scene records or `loadProjectAtomes(...)` followed by current Bevy scene state, and owns canonical preview persistence through `Atome.commit` props `preview_url`, `preview_width`, `preview_height`, and `preview_updated_at`; `bevy_project_preview_capture_adapter.js` retains the reusable capture iframe/canvas paintable behind the app with a cold-runtime timeout, and `bevy_project_preview_capture_frame.js` waits for deferred Bevy resources, rejects skipped/pending resources, and refuses transparent or nearly empty PNG captures. Default Dashboard boot uses persisted preview metadata only and does not hydrate missing project previews by loading projects in the background; preview capture belongs to explicit project/Matrix consumers or later non-boot refresh paths. These image records are disposable Dashboard projection fields, not canonical Atome properties, DOM backgrounds, per-item canvases, Matrix DOM clones, or symbolic fallbacks.
- Product-neutral rounded shape and drop-shadow support flows through `corner_radius` and `material.shadow` from render records to the Bevy projection adapter and Atome Bevy core. Rounded shape masks are generated by `atome/renderers/bevy-core/src/texture.rs` through the shared `spawn.rs` texture-handle helper used by both initial scenes and later spawn ops; disposable GPU drop shadows are generated by `atome/renderers/bevy-core/src/shape_shadow_overlay.rs`, keep the shape `AtomeCornerRadius` and owner opacity as runtime component state, reuse shared cached shadow textures through `AtomeShapeShadowTextureCache`, and use the same rounded-rectangle signed-distance geometry as an exterior silhouette mask with a transparent interior, so `material.shadow` starts at the card contour without changing the owner while radius-less shapes keep rectangular shadows. The drop shadow is composited below its owner and does not sample or blur the backdrop. `components.rs`, `spawn.rs`, `render_ops.rs`, and `resource_ops.rs` keep base visual color separate from opacity so style opacity patches remain linear and non-cumulative across shape, image, texture-backed text, resource patches, and `TextColor` records; `resource_ops.rs` owns Bevy resource-patch texture replacement and video external-texture resource refreshes while `render_ops.rs` remains the operation dispatcher. `shape_shadow_overlay_tests.rs` owns these focused Rust guardrails, while `backdrop_blur_tests.rs` owns the dormant scene-effect blur guardrail split out of the general Bevy core tests. Dashboard cards use this contract with the central dashboard card radius defaulting to `0` for square cells and matching each visual lane entête color while the lane body is derived from that entête with `laneShadePercent: -10`; dashboard entêtes use one external `header_side_shadow` image/texture gradient through the existing Bevy image path so the single lateral shadow reaches the tracks without a full-width shadow surface, drop-shadow blur, or interior band over the entête column.
- Shape-shadow overlay sprites opt out of Bevy automatic batching. They are transparent generated textures below their owner records, and keeping them in separate draw items prevents Safari/WebGPU first-frame batching from masking later Dashboard header text/icon textures after card shadows and lane darkening are present.
- The visible main menu and Atome contextual-edit rail are BevyUI-only. `bevy_ui_menu_surface.js` owns their shared tool constructor and stable shadow/surface/shell/content layer roles: standard horizontal and vertical backgrounds share one plane, while moving palette children use the single lower emergence role instead of index-derived layers. The contextual rail is a transparent scroll layout and never owns a second material or shadow behind its tools. The main menu Atome item calls `activateAtomShortPress({ source })` to close the active assistant or open the expertise-selected Dashboard, while `tool.main.home` remains a normal `ui.home.panel` route. The former DOM ribbon/footer renderer and its private helpers are deleted.
- `eVe/domains/dashboard/dashboard_bevy_ui_runtime.js` owns the canonical Bevy UI dashboard path. It reuses `dashboard_data_controller.js`, `dashboard_data_adapters.js`, `dashboard_layout.js`, `dashboard_environment_watcher.js`, `dashboard_tokens.js`, and `buildDashboardRecords()` to build one disposable Bevy UI tree for the neutral dashboard workspace; each Bevy UI node carries the canonical dashboard record for exact WebGPU overlay projection under the `__eve_bevy_ui_` prefix. The static phase mounts through `createEveBevyUiRuntime({ nativeUiEnabled: false })` so the visible path is the single overlay projection and native Bevy UI does not double-render over it before the interaction phases deliberately opt in. It exposes `window.eveDashboardBevyUiRuntime` and must not write legacy `__eve_dashboard_` records or create DOM dashboard nodes.
- `dashboard_bevy_ui_runtime.js` also owns the progressive open phase flags: `dashboard_bevy_ui_tree.js` filters the same canonical record list to shapes for the first mount, then `schedulePostOpenHydration()` completes category/preview hydration before one complete-tree mount. `bevy_ui_project_overlay_runtime.js` preserves mounted ids only for that transition and submits the newly added detail records, avoiding a redundant shape replacement. Slow image or text textures cannot block the structural Dashboard or workspace readiness, and these flags are transient projection state rather than canonical business state.
- `bevy_ui_project_overlay_runtime.js` forces overlay projection only when `previousIds` is empty. Mounted Dashboard updates therefore reuse `project_scene_direct_transform_runtime.js` for pure position changes during wheel, inertia, and snap frames; the main-menu tree remains prefix-atomic while live updates submit only changed, added, and removed records.
- `project_scene_prefix_reconciliation.js` owns prefix-id collection and the pure desired/changed/removal plan with identical workspace normalization, while `project_scene_runtime.js` only applies that plan; direct-transform comparison is therefore reachable without growing the scene owner. `dashboard_layout.js` exposes visible `lanes` for interaction plus stable `projection_lanes` for every category; offscreen lanes stay outside the canvas until scrolled into view, and partially visible lanes retain full logical size while their clip changes. `dashboard_header_records.js` and `dashboard_card_records.js` keep the root viewport clip separate from the exact visible-cell clip applied to child icons/media/text, preserving both exterior shadows and inter-row isolation. `project_scene_direct_transform_runtime.js` accepts position, logical-size, and clip changes through the existing projection path.
- `eVe/intuition/tools/workspace_main_menu_visibility.js` owns the post-auth/workspace invariant that the internally registered BevyUI main menu is mounted, visible, and projected into the active or transition-target scene. It awaits the current canonical menu render queue before checking projection: a catalog publication that supersedes the first mount must not fail workspace admission or leave the menu at its transition opacity. `user_workspace_surface_runtime.js` opens the Dashboard above the resident project surface and closes it back to that same surface without loading the project again. None of these owners creates a browser menu alias, DOM dashboard, or alternate canvas.
- `eVe/intuition/tools/project_bootstrap.js` owns delayed post-auth project restoration and readiness. It resolves the authenticated user's persisted current project before another authorized project, creates one `untitled` project only when none exists, persists that selection, synchronizes the active-project flag, and resolves its exported readiness only after `loadProjectAtomes()` completes. Dashboard startup keeps this prepared project resident; it must not clear its host, move `eve_surface_project` back to `#view`, or delete `window.__currentProject`.
- `eVe/intuition/tools/user_login_voice.js` owns login-sequence voice helper calls to the existing `Squirrel.voice` / `AtomeVoice` API. The pre-auth choice screen asks the localized voice-guidance prompt only through that voice API after `eve:login-choice-visible`; it must not call `speechSynthesis` directly or create a parallel TTS owner.
- `eVe/intuition/tools/user_accessibility_preferences_model.js` owns pure accessibility normalization. `eVe/intuition/runtime/bevy_panel/bevy_panel_home_view.js` composes the auditory/visual controls; `eVe/intuition/runtime/bevy_panel/bevy_panel_home_runtime.js` dispatches their existing profile update intents.
- `tests/probes/dashboard_bevy_runtime_probe.probe.mjs` owns the real Playwright dashboard acceptance path: guest workspace, boot-open-aware dashboard readiness, BevyUI Atome menu-item canvas clicks when needed, canvas header clicks, project item activation, fullscreen detail projection, toolbox reservation, handedness mirroring, dashboard teardown, screenshot checks for focused uniform backdrop, entêtes/card contrast/no dashboard paint inside the toolbox band, absence of `+` rail records, Dashboard project-preview pixels, project/contact media rounded-corner clipping, image-card label backdrop records/pixels, and browser-error checks covering console, page errors, request failures, and HTTP response failures. Its focused helpers live under `tests/probes/dashboard_bevy_runtime/`: `runtime_support.mjs` owns Playwright waits/click helpers, `snapshot_support.mjs` owns dashboard runtime/record snapshots, `visual_support.mjs` owns shared screenshot color assertions, and `project_preview_support.mjs` owns the canonical preview/contact-photo fixtures plus Dashboard card media/pixel assertions.
- `tests/probes/project_surface_reload_support.mjs` owns shared large-viewport Dashboard reload diagnostics: direct 1681px startup, no pre-diagnostic resize, project/canvas/surface/Bevy/header record snapshots, header-pixel screenshot checks, and optional post-capture resize comparison. `project_surface_resize_probe.test.mjs` is the Chromium non-regression runner over that support, while `project_surface_safari_reload_probe.test.mjs` is the WebKit/Safari runner and the only maintained automated probe for the Safari-specific reload-before-resize failure.
- `tests/probes/dashboard_workspace_stress_probe.probe.mjs` owns the deep real Playwright stress path for post-auth dashboard/project/Atome regressions: it creates an isolated authenticated user/session, reloads through the normal workspace bootstrap, creates an isolated project namespace including one Atome-only project plus three media projects, writes text/shape Atomes through `window.Atome.commitBatch`, imports image/audio/video through the existing project drop creator, verifies canonical `state_current`, Bevy scene IDs, media node kinds, zero visible dashboard records, one project canvas, zero per-Atome DOM hosts, repeated dashboard open/close, 30 header clicks, project-card switching, dashboard reopen after switching to an Atome-only project, viewport resize after switch, dynamic Calendar/Contacts invalidation, and the workspace interaction invariant: main menu visible, active project layer/canvas interactive, Mystic available, and real canvas drag still working after reopen/switch. Its focused helpers live under `tests/probes/dashboard_workspace_stress/`: `dashboard_cycles.mjs` owns startup-only Dashboard checks, real main-handle open/close cycles, fade-start/fade-settle timing, and page diagnostics; `dashboard_color_assertions.mjs` owns Dashboard color projection assertions inside Playwright page evaluation; `product_actions.mjs` owns project creation, media import, workspace interaction invariant checks, and real canvas drags; `support.mjs` owns waits, authenticated workspace entry, snapshots, screenshots, and report plumbing. The probe writes failure screenshots plus JSON reports under `temp/probe_reports/dashboard_workspace_stress/`.
- `tests/eve/dashboard_mockup_design_contract.test.mjs` owns the maintained non-browser contract against stable `eVe/R&D/dashboard_design.html` constants: initial no-focus state, header ratio, absence of a focused plus strip, `300ms` expansion timing, and category palette.
- `tests/eve/dashboard_render_performance_contract.test.mjs` owns the non-browser dashboard render budget and contract checks: visible-item bounded layout, exact simple/double snap points, pixel-snapped flush entêtes, square shadowed card interiors, handedness mirroring, painted-toolbox reservation over transparent wrappers, live environment signature watching, preference update propagation, compact high-density image icons, one-record text labels through higher-density `text_style`, single external texture-gradient `header_side_shadow` projection with no `material.shadow`, dashboard project veil, no Dashboard mask in the toolbox reserved band, bottom texture shadow, no default `backdrop_blur` emission, cache-first category activation, absence of focused plus records, and dashboard diff/scheduler coalescing. `tests/eve/dashboard_layout.probe.mjs` guards fixed block-unit sizing from the maintoolbox square size, non-stretched lanes, bottom-lane alignment above the toolbox band, HTML-overflow-style horizontal max clamping and final snap endpoint behavior, whole-block horizontal viewport quantization, overflow-safe hit testing, active-category item deduplication, distribution across lanes, item hit-test category ownership, label-edit rect alignment, and absence of active-plus routing. `tests/eve/dashboard_interaction_runtime.probe.mjs` guards smooth continuous horizontal/vertical interaction offsets, ignored horizontal scroll when all items fit, clamped wheel/pointer horizontal offsets, physical horizontal scroll direction, and entête-side final snap anchoring for both handedness modes. `tests/eve/dashboard_focus_color_contract.test.mjs` guards unfocused multi-color rubrique projection, focused active-category uniform backdrop across dashboard chrome, entête-matched cards, overview lane darkening, and active-header toggle restoration.
- `dashboard_header_records.js` owns the two-tier rail geometry: the upper band (icon + rubrique label, both bounded by the band height), the lower band (`eve.dashboard.header.new`), the `2 px` `header_separator_*` line and the full-frame `header_bg_*` glass, all derived from `dashboard_layout.js#dashboardHeaderBands`. `dashboard_bevy_ui_runtime.js` owns its single minute-aligned timer and lifecycle cleanup. `tests/eve/dashboard_header_zones.test.mjs` guards the band partition, the split-line hit zones, the separator and `Nouveau` records, the upper-band content bounds and the filter cascade; `tests/eve/dashboard_records.test.mjs` keeps its geometry guard but is red on pre-existing expectations (the Calendar time/date records and the removed label contract no longer exist in the records); `shape_shadow_overlay_tests.rs` guards repeated style rebuilds without live-overlay accumulation.
- `eVe/intuition/runtime/eve_intuition/main_menu_content_runtime.js` routes the Home tool to the canonical user/Home panel. It must not call the dashboard runtime; active-workspace dashboard toggling belongs to the visible ribbon handle path, which must call `toggleWorkspaceDashboardAndMainMenu()` rather than the low-level Dashboard runtime so missing neutral-scene projection is repaired before any close decision.
- `eVe/intuition/tools/core/tool_runtime_bootstrap_defs_b.js` declares the dashboard News, Monitor, Goals, and future Store tool contracts. Runtime handlers are registered by `eVe/domains/dashboard/dashboard_bevy_ui_runtime.js` in the existing `window.atome.tools.handlers` registry.

## Explicit Atome Open / eVe Closed Boundary

The source tree is split by ownership, not by convenience:

- `atome/`, `server/`, and `database/` are open framework or infrastructure surfaces only when the code remains product-neutral.
- `eVe/` is the closed product layer for UI, product tools, workflows, branding, product stores, Molecule/MTraX behavior, and private composition.
- `tests/` may cross both layers only to validate contracts, integration, and regression behavior.
- `temp/` is the only approved location for temporary diagnostics, probes, and generated transient outputs.

Dependency rule:

- Open Atome code may not import closed eVe UI, product workflows, private tools, branding, or product stores.
- When open Atome code needs a closed product capability, the capability must be injected, registered, or exposed through an explicit runtime boundary documented in `maps/API_MAP.md` and `maps/ARCHITECTURE_MAP.md`.
- eVe may consume Atome open contracts, but it must not duplicate Atome security, sync, server, database, audio, communication, AI, MCP, or cross-platform framework services.
- Product-specific code must stay in `eVe/` unless it is deliberately promoted to Atome through an open contract, tests, and map updates.

Placement rule:

- Framework APIs, reusable runtime contracts, security, sync, server, database, audio, voice orchestration, and MCP primitives belong to Atome/open infrastructure.
- Product UI, panel chrome, tool composition, Finder-facing workflows, Matrix, ribbon, mystic, closed voice surfaces, product persistence adapters, and Molecule/MTraX workflows belong to eVe.
- Existing bootstrap touchpoints from Atome into eVe are boundary debt and must be verified before structural changes rather than normalized as generic dependency permission.

### Atome Open Layer

Path: `atome/`

Role: Open framework, reusable runtime, cross-platform infrastructure, security, server-facing contracts, Squirrel APIs, AI/voice contracts, and generic assets.

Main responsibilities:

- Core Squirrel framework and public API surface.
- Cross-platform runtime bootstrap for browser, Tauri, iOS, AUv3, and FreeBSD targets.
- Shared security and synchronization primitives that must remain product-neutral.
- Audio runtime contracts and native backend boundaries.
- AI, voice, mail, contacts, calendar, bank, and MCP-facing contracts when they are product-neutral.

Should be extended by:

- Framework-level APIs and runtime contracts.
- Open security, sync, server, and cross-platform modules.
- Generic components that are not eVe product UI.

Should not contain:

- Closed eVe product UI.
- eVe workflows, branding, private tool composition, or product-specific panels.
- Product-only MTraX/Molecule UI internals unless promoted through an explicit open contract.

Status: Verified by current tree inspection.

Adole mutation boundary:

- `atome/src/squirrel/apis/unified/adole_api/atomes.js` owns public `AdoleAPI.atomes.*` compatibility methods.
- `atome/src/squirrel/apis/unified/adole_api/atome_record_projection.js` owns browser-side Atome API record projection and must keep network/storage aliases at the boundary instead of re-emitting them as public record shape.
- `atome/src/squirrel/apis/unified/adole_api/auth_fastify_token.js` owns Fastify token validity state, including clearing stale `window.__SQUIRREL_FASTIFY_AUTH_INVALID__` after an authenticated Fastify session is proven. `auth_methods_session_account.js` owns anonymous workspace bootstrap and must reject Fastify anonymous bootstrap responses that do not install a real token/session; `auth_methods_login.js` marks successful Fastify login/register/bootstrap results through the same token-validity owner.
- `atome/src/squirrel/apis/unified/adole.js` owns detached WebSocket adapters, including `adapter.atome.commit` and `adapter.atome.commitBatch`.
- Framework code must route durable Atome writes through the event commit surface even when the public method name is still `create` or `alter`.

### eVe Closed Layer

Path: `eve/`

Role: Closed product layer containing eVe UI, product tools, workflows, composition, branding, persistence adapters, and product-specific media/Molecule/MTraX runtime.

Main responsibilities:

- eVe application bootstrap and product composition.
- Intuition UI, panels, tools, menu, ribbon, mystic, matrix, and product interaction runtime.
- Closed media domains and MTraX/Molecule product workflows.
- Product-specific event stores, project stores, media stores, browser/iOS/Tauri storage adapters.
- Product i18n and design systems.

Should be extended by:

- eVe UI, panels, product tools, product workflows, branding, and closed runtime bridges.
- Product-specific media editing, MTraX/Molecule panels, and private persistence flows.

Should not contain:

- Open Atome framework primitives that must be reused outside the eVe product.
- Generic security or cross-platform server contracts that belong in Atome.

Status: Verified by current tree inspection.

Capture reveal ownership:

- `eVe/intuition/tools/capture_reveal_runtime.js` owns how a capture enters the project: it forwards a reserved atome id to persistence (`createPendingCaptureExportOptions`) and lands a new top-level sound from the recording tool onto its canonical place (`landCaptureAtome`, presentation-only direct scene motion ending on the canonical record; natural view only). The former DOM extraction animation and `capture_export_geometry.js` were removed.
- `eVe/intuition/tools/capture_source_runtime.js` owns capture source/action tool resolution, source-tool memory, capture invocation input normalization, capture-menu detection, and capture result Atome id extraction for `capture.js`.
- `eVe/intuition/tools/capture_recording_feedback_runtime.js` owns bounded Bevy tool feedback and post-stop reveal delegation for `capture.js`. It receives the invoking Bevy presentation (`mystic` or `main_menu`) and projects one session only to that surface. During a main-menu capture, `bevy_ui_main_menu_model.js` preserves the complete menu; an audio take replaces the tool content with its live scope, and a camera take only turns the tool icon red (`action_indicator`) while the large preview panel shows the camera. A camera take previewed in the project Visual also turns the ribbon tool red. Mystic closes after starting capture; the existing recording owner retains the session and feedback. The main-menu recording runtime keeps the matching session/sequence (and the drawn scope across phase changes of the same take), splits each scope frame into ~40 ms history columns, deduplicates unchanged envelopes, and caps their projection at 30 Hz through motion patches that move and resize each column (`patchBevyUiTreeMotion` honours `style.size`); `bevy_ui_main_menu_recording_visual_model.js` draws contiguous ~2 px square columns spanning each signed min/max with linear amplitude, a 2 px centre line in silence, and keeps the frozen scope through `stopping`/`persisting`. These owners register live sources through their renderer owners but create no audio-scope canvas, frozen media clone, camera element, visible media node, or second renderer.
- `eVe/intuition/tools/capture_video_recording_runtime.js` owns only the serialized generic video start/stop lifecycle and its retryable feedback transitions for `capture.js`. The former quick-record module name and its unused long-press session, document-level pointer/click binding, click suppression, and iOS pointer interception branches were removed; BevyUI tool dispatch is the interaction owner.
- The unconsumed capture fullscreen/expanded DOM branch was deleted: `capture_fullscreen_runtime.js`, `capture_fullscreen_chrome_runtime.js`, `capture_expanded_surface_dom.js`, and `intuition/runtime/preview_surface.js` are not product capture owners and must not be restored as a visible preview or alternate interaction path.
- `eVe/intuition/tools/capture.js` owns capture tool action registration, menu registration, and controller dispatch only; source resolution, feedback lifecycle, serialized video start/stop, and media reveal are delegated to focused runtimes. It does not compose an audio/video DOM preview.
- `eVe/intuition/tools/core/tool_runtime_recording_handlers.js` owns the BevyUI-tool-to-recorder bridge for capture and detail-record actions, including Molecule delegation and idempotent on/off routing. It must reach the registered `capture.js` handler and the real audio/video controllers; a latched visual result is not evidence that recording started.
- `eVe/domains/media/api/audio_api.js` owns generic product audio start/stop/persistence and installs `createMoleculeRecordingCaptureAdapter` over that existing controller. `eVe/domains/media/api/video_recording_controller.js` owns real generic video session start/stop state and capture cleanup, with no preview-stream renderer. Generic recording remains available independently of the narrower exact-overdub capability.
- `atome/engines/audio-engine/src/metering_scope.rs` publishes a lock-free fixed 64-bin scope snapshot from the existing CPAL recorder pass; `atome/src/application/audio_runtime/record_audio_scope_transport.js` registers the session before polling `audio_get_scope` at 34 ms, retains only the newest matching frame for late-subscriber replay, and dispatches ephemeral `native_audio_scope` frames. `audio_core_record.js` replays that frame once and rejects stale sequences. The snapshot is visualization data, not a second recorder or persisted state.
- `eVe/domains/rendering/project_scene_invalidation_runtime.js` owns derived selection and audio-playback-progress invalidation. It targets only the foreground scene and delegates to `tryApplyDirectEphemeralStyles()` in `project_scene_direct_style_runtime.js`; these events emit style-only patches and may not rebuild the scene or touch video resources, textures, or decoder ownership. A full render is a single bounded initialization fallback only.
- `eVe/domains/media/api/video_api_record.js` owns browser/native video start-stop orchestration, retryable persistence/project association, and terminal controller retention. `video_api_record_options.js` owns exact-request detection plus file/option normalization, and `video_api_record_finalize.js` owns browser terminal payload validation (non-empty bytes, positive duration, and a real video MIME result). `video_api_record_native.js` owns bounded iOS native commands, recovery from host state after WebView reload, idempotent stop/cancel, required-audio probing, and terminal acknowledgement only after durable project association. These modules are one controller path, not alternate recorders.
- `eVe/domains/media/api/audio_core_record.js` keeps a native controller in `finalization_failed` when native timing validation is terminal and exposes explicit discard recovery. `audio_core_storage.js` owns the corresponding physical-file deletion through `AtomeFileSystem.deleteFile`; a failed or timed-out deletion stays retryable and must not be reported as discarded.
- `eVe/intuition/tools/molecule/recording/index.js` owns Molecule armed-track validation, exact capability/start/stop/cancel/dispose, integer-frame clip creation, and commit through the active Molecule session. `eVe/intuition/tools/molecule/runtime.js` owns its group timeline API exposure and adapter injection; `kernel/time_model.js` and `kernel/schemas.js` own frame projections and persisted recording timing validation.
- A completed exact capture is cached inside the Molecule recording coordinator until `session.apply("molecule.clip.add", clip)` succeeds. A failed clip commit moves the coordinator to `commit_failed`; retrying `stop()` reuses the same validated capture and durable media Atome instead of stopping or persisting the backend twice.
- `eVe/intuition/runtime/project_media_import_runtime.js` owns shared project media file selection for mystic-menu import, capture import, and the Home/Background wallpaper import, emits the narrow Xcode-visible import trace for iOS/AUv3 picker diagnosis, then delegates durable project media creation to `project_drop.importFilesToProjectViaCreator`. The picker order is fixed: native iOS/AUv3 document-picker bridge, then `showOpenFilePicker`, then one transient `<input type=file>` appended to `document.body` and removed on settle. `tools/background.js#openBackgroundImportDialog` calls requestProjectImportFiles({ accept: image/*, multiple: false }) and hands the file to `importBackgroundImageFile`, so no panel or dialog keeps its own hidden file input. The native request declares its source: an image-only `accept` travels as the `photos` token and is answered by the host's Apple Photos picker (`platforms/ios/atome-auv3/Common/PhotoLibraryImportPicker.swift`), an undeclared import (empty `accept` or `*`) travels as the `*` token and is answered by the host's Photos/Fichiers source choice, and any other vocabulary keeps the document picker; `multiple` now selects single or multiple selection in both pickers.
- `eVe/intuition/runtime/eve_intuition/panel_tool_registration_runtime.js` owns eVeIntuition panel tool registration for home/contact/info/finder/communicate/delete/undo/paste/timeline/calendar/background/couleur/size/font/detail/layer.
- `eVe/intuition/runtime/eve_intuition/basic_ui_tools_runtime.js` owns the lightweight bootstrap registrations for undo, Play latch, select, circle, text creation, matrix view, orientation, palette visibility, and item enable/disable. The post-presentation phase in `boot_runtime.js` replaces the provisional Play latch exactly once with `media_reader_tool_runtime.js`; no global loader is exposed. The former hidden `dummy` catalog registration was removed because it was diagnostic/demo surface, not product runtime.
- `eVe/intuition/runtime/eve_intuition/main_tool_latched_state_runtime.js` owns main-tool latched state resolution across runtime APIs, cached latch state, menu alias families, visible panel surfaces, and disposable tool button visuals.
- `eVe/intuition/runtime/eve_intuition/main_tool_selection_guard_runtime.js` owns main-tool selection-required policy and no-target activation blocking for vector/record-action style tools.
- `eVe/intuition/runtime/eve_intuition/finder_tool_identity_runtime.js` owns Finder tool identity, target normalization, candidate collection, and projection-instance target recovery.
- `eVe/intuition/runtime/eve_intuition/finder_inline_visual_runtime.js` owns shared disposable inline input visual expansion/collapse styling for Finder-style main-ribbon inputs.
- `eVe/intuition/runtime/eve_intuition/finder_inline_runtime.js` owns Finder inline search orchestration, finder panel open/close/focus/refresh wrappers, and the Finder touch handler consumed by main tools, footer tools, and the tool window bridge.
- `eVe/intuition/ribbon/bevy_ui_product_runtime.js` routes both the Atome hold gesture and retained AI tool invocation to the same assistant runtime. The former DOM inline editor and direct AI action executor were removed.
- `eVe/intuition/runtime/eve_intuition/atome_contextual_rail_model_runtime.js` owns contextual-rail tool-key normalization, default tool sets by atome kind, persisted rail-tool read/write, project-playback tool availability, record-action mode/source normalization, and rail catalog tool definitions.
- `eVe/intuition/runtime/eve_intuition/atome_contextual_edit_runtime.js` owns session-only multi-Atome editing state, active context, canonical footer drag/resize gestures, fullscreen geometry, handedness, rail scroll, vertical-slider sessions, and BevyUI lifecycle. It consumes the canonical workspace-mode notification to unmount all edit chrome over Dashboard without losing the same-project session, remount it on return, and clear it when another project becomes active.
- `eVe/intuition/runtime/eve_intuition/atome_contextual_edit_model.js` owns the single shared-canvas BevyUI tree for edit outlines, per-Atome footers, fixed lateral rail, palette children, and 3x vertical slider reflow. It consumes the shared Bevy menu surface contract and renders palette accents on the rail interior: left for right-handed placement and right for left-handed placement.
- `eVe/intuition/runtime/eve_intuition/atome_contextual_edit_model.js` also owns the rail's pinned zone: `buildAtomeContextualEditTree({ activeSlots })` removes the moved entries from the ordinary level (`withoutMovedTools`) and reserves `min(n × itemSize, availableRailHeight)` against `layout.bottom`, so the lit cases sit above the Atome handle, never scroll with the rail viewport, and push the ordinary stack up by exactly their height. The pinned case keeps the level node id `atome_contextual_tool_<safeId(key)>` (`…_icon`), hence the same handler, tool identity and pulse. `atome_contextual_edit_runtime.js` owns the activation order (`state.activeSlotOrder`) and the `litToolIconIds()` list synchronized on the unique `createRecordingIndicatorPulse`. (2026-10-09: replaced by `createLitToolGlowPulse` — see ARCHITECTURE_MAP `### 2026-10-09 — Engaged tools glow`.)
- `eVe/intuition/runtime/eve_intuition/atome_contextual_edit_registry.js` owns the closed module registry replacing the deleted `window.eveAtomeEditFooterApi` exposure.
- `eVe/intuition/runtime/eve_intuition/atome_contextual_rail_definition_invocation_runtime.js` owns contextual-rail definition invocation for its runtime and explicit context calls, including selection-bound payload merge and slider dispatch.
- All capture action/runtime files are below the 500-line hard maximum after R23.

Perform tool ownership:

- `eVe/intuition/tools/perform.js` owns only perform-mode lifecycle orchestration (activate/deactivate), pointer gating for the view-only context, tool-handler registration, and the public `window.evePerformApi` control surface; the removed dead `window.evePerformDebug` test surface and the no-op `logPerformDebug` tracing must not be reintroduced.

Selection-style tool ownership:

- `eVe/intuition/tools/selection_style_apply.js` owns the public `applyColorToSelection`/`applySizeToSelection`/`applyFontToSelection`/`readSelectionCount` facade consumed by the couleur/size/font tools, plus the re-exported `hasTextStyleRangeSelectionIntent`/`resolveCurrentTextSizeValue`. For active or recently blurred project text selections it routes the remembered non-empty range into the canonical `rich_text` mutation; otherwise it applies the canonical whole-Atome property. It never derives or persists business spans from DOM state.
- `eVe/domains/rendering/rich_text_style.js` owns deterministic rich-text normalization, range split/merge, and per-index style lookup for `bold`, `color`, `font_family`, and numeric `font_size`. Text layout, texture rasterization, caret, and selection geometry consume this owner so persisted spans and WebGPU projection cannot diverge.
- `eVe/intuition/tools/selection_style_state.js` owns the text-selection memory / style-intent mutable state singletons and the timing/selector constants; `selection_style_ranges.js` owns the stateless DOM-range serialization and numeric/font-size helpers.
- `eVe/intuition/tools/selection_style_context.js` owns the selection target resolvers (selection ids, focused/editing text host, text-style intent, host elements, style-tool interaction targets); `selection_style_text_selection.js` owns the live/remembered editable and preview text-range contexts and the current text-size resolution.
- `eVe/intuition/tools/selection_style_memory.js` owns capturing/restoring the active text range selection for style tools and the selectionchange/pointerdown bridge.
- `eVe/intuition/tools/selection_style_atome.js` owns canonical Atome state/property reads, media/text classification, the canonical property update path, and per-selection mutation resolution/application. A selected structural Molecule resolves through canonical `parent_id` ancestry to every live non-Molecule descendant, recursively and without painting transparent Molecule owners; explicit Size remains on its existing recursive transform route. `selection_style_svg_layer.js` owns selected project SVG-layer color application through that update path.
- `eVe/intuition/tools/core/active_tool_registry.js` owns the residence of the tools that remain lit once started (`rail` | `menu`) and the invocation path used both to read and to stop them; `readActiveToolEntries({ keepToolId })` lists the lit menu-resident entries and `stopActiveToolEntries({ keepToolId })` stops them through the method each tool already publishes before releasing its latch. `tool_runtime_circle_tool_state.stopOperativeCreationTools` delegates to it; no other surface re-decides a lit tool's residence.

Finder tool ownership:

- `eVe/intuition/tools/finder.js` owns only the disposable finder dialog skeleton DOM (search row, scope buttons, result header/list) and header-sort wiring; it populates the `finderEls` handle registry and is the lazy-import entry from `panel_definitions.js`. The removed dead `toErrorMessage`/`isTauriRuntime`/`getCurrentProjectId`/`logFinderToolsSources` helpers and the undefined `extractAtomeFromGetResult` people-lookup fallback must not be reintroduced.
- Finder state and categories are owned by `eVe/intuition/runtime/bevy_panel/bevy_panel_finder_runtime.js` and `eVe/intuition/runtime/bevy_panel/bevy_panel_finder_model.js`. Pure records and drag payloads remain in `eVe/intuition/tools/finder_record_model.js` and `eVe/intuition/tools/finder_record_projection.js`.
- `eVe/intuition/tools/finder_data_sources.js` owns the API/runtime tool-record loaders and the tool-registry divergence self-heal; `finder_refresh.js` owns local/project/tools/people record refresh into finderState and the atome-change event bus.
- `eVe/intuition/runtime/bevy_panel/bevy_panel_finder_view.js` composes shared Bevy controls; `eVe/intuition/runtime/bevy_panel/bevy_panel_finder_interactions.js` owns their intents. `eVe/intuition/runtime/bevy_panel/bevy_panel_finder_runtime.js` owns refresh, selection, transfer and panel lifecycle. The former DOM Finder view/controller is retired.

Detail tool ownership (in progress under R26):

- Historical snapshot (retired owner; not current availability evidence): `retired:eVe/intuition/tools/detail.js` owns only the detail panel facade: open/close lifecycle, polling, current-detail synchronization, toolbar button wiring, and window exposure.
- Historical snapshot (retired owner; not current availability evidence): `retired:eVe/intuition/tools/detail_actions.js` owns registration of the `ui.detail.karaoke.apply`, `ui.detail.record.toggle`, and `ui.detail.timecodes.toggle` UI actions while preserving the existing tool-gateway route.
- Historical snapshot (retired owner; not current availability evidence): `retired:eVe/intuition/tools/detail_state.js` owns detail panel constants, shared mutable panel state, record-action mirroring, preview source normalization, formatting, and host/footer API readers.
- Historical snapshot (retired owner; not current availability evidence): `retired:eVe/intuition/tools/detail_view.js` owns disposable detail-panel DOM construction and rendering for preview, summary, SVG layers, take composites, empty state, and toolbar projection.
- Historical snapshot (retired owner; not current availability evidence): `retired:eVe/intuition/tools/detail_context_tools.js` owns detail footer/context-tool rendering and footer tool-state synchronization.
- Historical snapshot (retired owner; not current availability evidence): `retired:eVe/intuition/tools/detail_editor_model.js` owns pure karaoke line payload normalization, rich/plain text conversion, and editor line id/markup helpers.
- Historical snapshot (retired owner; not current availability evidence): `retired:eVe/intuition/tools/detail_editor_runtime.js` owns the dormant lyrics/karaoke editor runtime: editable-state projection, line DOM normalization, line sync, line click/record interaction, paste/drop handling, and active-line scrolling.
- Historical snapshot (retired owner; not current availability evidence): `retired:eVe/intuition/tools/detail_styles.js` owns the detail panel base CSS injection (`ensureDetailStyles`) and concatenates the dormant karaoke/lyrics editor CSS from `detail_styles_karaoke.js`. The karaoke/lyrics editor stays dormant pending the Bevy karaoke host rebuild and must not be deleted as dead code.

Atome DOM projection ownership:

- `eVe/core/atome_dom_id.js` owns the final Atome DOM host id contract: `eve-atome_<atome_id>`, `toDomId`, `fromDomId`, `getAtomeElement`, nearest-host resolution helpers, and WeakMap-backed runtime metadata for projection-only elements.
- `eVe/intuition/matrix/core/project_dom_state.js` owns WeakMap-backed project projection metadata for project views, Matrix tiles, and project-scoped tool projection hosts; project identity must be recovered from this runtime registry or `project_view_<id>` ids, not from `data-project-id`.
- Final Atome DOM hosts and their final rendered subtrees must not carry Atome business state, type, selection, group, media, renderer, drag, resize, binding, project, replay, or persistence data in `data-*` attributes.
- Event layers such as click, double-click, selection, drag, resize, mystic menu routing, media transport, and MTRAX opening must resolve the nearest `eve-atome_*` host, recover the canonical Atome id, and then consult the Atome registry, runtime registry, or owning domain registry for behavior decisions.
- Binding flags and ephemeral UI state belong in runtime registries or WeakMaps. Group/media runtime facts belong in their owning runtime/domain registries, not in the DOM.
- Legacy read-only fallbacks discovered while migrating old DOM are removal debt. They
  must not be extended or treated as supported behavior. New and touched rendering code
  emits only the clean projection contract, and routing must not branch on Atome
  `data-*` state.

Unified rendering ownership:

- Rich-text raster measurement inside `bevy_media_texture_resolver.js` is paint-state neutral: temporary canvas font changes are restored, and every `fillText`/`strokeText` run explicitly reapplies its resolved family, size, and weight. This keeps half-open Font/Size ranges on their intended glyphs without overlap or style leakage to the following run.
- `eVe/domains/rendering/` owns the closed product unified rendering projection contract for eVe Atomes: disposable `RenderAtom` normalization, render-scene ordering and hit testing, bounded project/matrix canvas surfaces, the single active `text_editing_session.js` keyboard/selection/caret owner, glyph geometry from `text_editing_layout.js`, unitless-or-pixel line-height normalization from `bevy_text_texture_style.js`, hidden text service lifecycle, first-versus-active text double-click arbitration in `surface_runtime.js`, panel interaction interception, and the shared WebGPU render-at-time compositor entry point. `atome_contextual_edit_runtime.js` refreshes disposable contextual chrome when the hidden active text editor emits input so the footer follows live measured multiline geometry.
- `eVe/domains/rendering/render_atom.js` owns the disposable RenderAtom content projection and must consume the shared media source canonicalization contract before media reaches Bevy, so bare uploaded or recorded filenames become explicit `/api/uploads/...` or `/api/recordings/...` sources with owner scope instead of browser-relative URLs. Canonical SVG Atomes stay editable `shape` records, while an SVG source/MIME or inline `svg_markup` projects as disposable `image` content for the shared Bevy SVG texture resolver; an ordinary labelled shape must remain `shape`. Inline canonical markup takes projection priority over a stale original source after vector edits. The record's canonical owner overrides a stale URL query owner only in this derived projection. Video RenderAtom content also normalizes the derived timeline fields `start`, `duration`, `trimIn`, `trimOut`, `offset`, `speed`, and `loop` from canonical Atome properties for renderer/decode projection; this remains disposable projection data and must not become a writable renderer state store.
- `eVe/domains/rendering/virtual_scene_contract.js` owns the Phase 1 renderer-agnostic Virtual Scene contract: disposable `AtomeRenderNode` projection, deterministic tree construction, selected-state projection from the runtime selection table, dense render-layer assignment from the actual scene paint order, render diff operations, and dirty-flag names. It is a projection/diff contract only; it must not become an ECS, renderer, canonical Atome model, or DOM-backed state store.
- `eVe/domains/rendering/bevy_projection_adapter.js` owns the strict browser Bevy projection adapter from `AtomeRenderNode` Virtual Scene data into the `run_atome_bevy_renderer(...)` payload shape. It maps explicit id, kind, parent, logical position, logical size, local transform scale/rotation/origin, paint-order layer, opacity, selected flag, fill color, text, shape `material.shadow`, media source, media texture, media `uvRect` / pixel `sourceRect` crop data, video timeline projection, waveform peak fields, and project-audio waveform playback progress, normalizes direct CSS z-index/layer payloads to the Rust `i32` boundary when no Virtual Scene render layer exists, normalizes browser `Uint8ClampedArray` texture readbacks to `Uint8Array` before wasm-bindgen, rejects unsupported advanced Bevy blend modes instead of falling back to normal, validates video timeline numbers and speed before payload emission, and rejects incomplete projection nodes instead of inventing silent defaults. `eVe/domains/rendering/bevy_projection_style.js` owns reusable Bevy projection color and shadow normalization consumed by that adapter.
- `eVe/domains/rendering/renderer_adapter_registry.js` owns the pure renderer adapter registry contract: normalized kind ids, renderer names, immutable capability metadata, explicit registration, lookup, assertion, and list operations. It does not own DOM state, canonical Atome state, texture generation, or renderer dispatch side effects.
- `eVe/domains/rendering/bevy_renderer_adapter_registry.js` owns the default Bevy renderer adapter declarations and kind-specific node/resource mapping callbacks for the currently supported `shape`, `text`, `image`, `video`, and `audio_waveform` kinds. `bevy_projection_adapter.js` owns common Bevy payload validation/base fields and delegates kind-specific projection to the registered adapter.
- `eVe/domains/rendering/bevy_pending_media_contract.js` owns the browser Bevy contract for source-backed media whose disposable texture is not available at spawn time. It identifies persisted video poster and waveform peak inputs that must be consumed during initial projection, requires image nodes to resolve a texture before spawn, and marks uncached video plus waveform media with transparent material so Rust never exposes its default blue shape while the same Bevy resource path generates the delayed texture.
- `eVe/domains/rendering/bevy_media_texture_resolver.js` owns disposable browser texture decoding for the Bevy web route and for presentable native handoff when the native renderer runtime is explicitly injected with the shared resolver. It converts text, raster images, SVG documents, persisted video posters, and audio waveforms into explicit RGBA texture payloads without adding visible media DOM nodes and without rendering through a legacy project adapter. Oversized targets retain their logical dimensions through fit calculation, then use one shared scale below `1` when necessary to bound both texture axes without changing aspect ratio; independently clamping width and height is forbidden. Text textures consume structured `text_style` fields for font size, font weight, alignment, baseline, padding, stroke, and opt-in shrink fitting so labels do not need duplicate shadow/outline records or clipped text records. `bevy_text_texture_style.js` owns shared text style/rich-text line helpers, `bevy_text_texture_fit.js` owns deterministic width fitting, `bevy_text_texture_pixels.js` owns transparent text-edge pixel bleeding for linear WebGPU sampling, and `bevy_media_texture_cache.js` owns byte-bounded reuse of generated text/image/SVG texture payloads by content, rich-text edit/selection state, style, dimensions, DPR, and scale. The cache deduplicates concurrent resolution of one key, exposes in-flight diagnostics, and advances a generation on clear so an obsolete pending decode cannot repopulate a cleared cache. `bevy_ui_image_runtime.js` preserves the final hydrated/tinted result as a typed RGBA payload (`Uint8ClampedArray` until boundary normalization), avoiding `Array` expansion and repeated tint/raster work. Text and image cache keys intentionally exclude the render node id so stable content survives Dashboard slot/layout movement. Source-backed live project video without a persisted poster rejects with `bevy_media_texture_video_gpu_source_only` before `drawImage` or `getImageData`, so the RGBA resolver cannot become the live playback path. Source-backed audio waveforms may decode source audio to derive real peaks before texture generation.
- `eVe/domains/rendering/bevy_surface_background_runtime.js` owns browser Bevy project-surface background delivery. It consumes the `eve:surface-background-changed` event from the user background preference path, decodes image backgrounds to cached RGBA texture payloads when needed, normalizes browser `Uint8ClampedArray` pixels to the `Uint8Array` required by the Rust `Vec<u8>` boundary, forwards generated textures through the same normalization, and calls `apply_atome_bevy_surface_background` so the Rust Bevy scene renders background pixels before Atomes and text. `atome/renderers/bevy-core/src/background.rs` keeps the quad exactly equal to the logical viewport and computes a centered source `Rect` for `cover`; resize updates both values, so Dashboard and project wallpapers fill the surface without aspect-ratio deformation or oversized background geometry. Applied signatures are cached per canvas and current renderer state: duplicate delivery to one renderer is suppressed, while a replacement renderer on the same canvas receives the latest background. `surface_background_defaults.js` owns the shared default surface color contract used by Bevy background fallback and the HTML shell CSS variable `--eve-default-surface-background`; the solid/no-pattern generated default must emit a color-only patch, not a transparent generated texture. `user_background_pattern_renderer.js` owns generated background pixel production, while `user_surface_background_texture_runtime.js` owns preference-to-surface payload orchestration for `eVe/user/background.js`; `eVe/user/background.js` exports the strict `startUserBackgroundRuntime()` boot hook instead of starting at module import, and `eVe/eVe.js` invokes it immediately after `eve.user_background` loads so missing `#view` remains an explicit startup error without making the module import itself fail. `eVe/user/background.js` also owns protected-image authorization by resolved URL owner: local Axum media uses `local_auth_token`, while absolute Fastify media uses `cloud_auth_token`; it consumes `eve:profile-preferences-updated` so background preference changes from the existing user-panel route are projected immediately and local wallpaper choices are not overwritten by a stale profile read before the saved profile catches up. It never polls the profile (2026-10-09: the former 1.2 s poll re-read the whole profile, photo included, ~210 KB per read on iOS): it re-reads on `squirrel:auth-checked` and when the page becomes visible, and applies `eve:user-profile-updated` and the owner's synchronized `squirrel:atome-updated`/`-restored` profile patch directly. Generated Background panel parameters remain owned by the existing Background panel modules, and a generated edit reclaims the render from a selected image: `background_prefs.js#applyBackgroundParams` adds `backgroundSource: 'generated'` when the patch declares neither `backgroundSource` nor `backgroundImageUrl`, so the pattern/colour controls are not silently swallowed by the bundled default image. `user_surface_background_texture_runtime.js` retains the profile wallpaper across workspace navigation; the existing workspace-mode event changes only its project-colour cover, transparent for Dashboard and full-surface Dashboard Matrix. The shared default colour is `#a9a9a9` (RGB 169/169/169) in both `surface_background_defaults.js` and `--eve-default-surface-background`. `user_surface_background_texture_runtime.js` also projects the project own colour, which is canonical model state and not a rendering concern: it subscribes to the shared `atome:changed` event on `core/event_bus.js`, reads the project record only through `window.Atome.getStateCurrent()` (read-only, no DOM mirror, no second store), takes the first declared property among `background`, `backgroundColor` and `bg`, converts it with the shared `readSurfaceBackgroundRgba()` from `surface_background_defaults.js` (`#rgb`/`#rrggbb`, `rgb()`/`rgba()`, alpha clamped to 0..1, unparsable values resolve to `null`) and projects it as the existing background descriptor's RGBA `cover`, retaining its media signature, texture and source URL. Projection is gated on the current project identity; a project without a colour uses the shared default cover. The colour value is never written here - the `couleur` tool of the project contextual rail writes `{ background, backgroundColor, bg }` through `applyColorToSelection` and the canonical commit pipeline. `start()` owns the `atome:changed` subscription and `stop()` releases it, so an imported but unstarted module still paints nothing. `eVe/user/background.js` exports `defaultUserBackgroundParams()`, which resolves a profile owner without a stored `preferences.background` to `assets/images/Background/eVe.png`; that resolution is applied to the live params without `schedulePreferenceSave`, so the guest, first-launch and new-account default is never persisted. The explicit user `download` wallpaper action delegates remote image retrieval and upload registration to `downloadRemoteWallpaper`, which posts only to the resolved Fastify `/api/uploads/remote-wallpaper` owner, then persists the resulting protected media URL as the selected background. `downloadRemoteWallpaper` owns the whole reliability contract of that action: one download at a time (a second click shares the request in flight), the inline `?inline=1` answer for a session that owns nothing on the server, a single re-minted-token replay on 401, a single replay on 429, and typed `{ ok: false, reason }` failures. A background that is applied but not written to the profile — the guest case, which has no profile — is reported by `background_image.js` as `{ ok: true, persisted: false }`, not as a failed action. A video wallpaper is paused while the applied cover is opaque (a project's own colour hides it) and resumed when uncovered; URL, decoder and playhead are kept.
- `eVe/domains/rendering/bevy_web_renderer_runtime.js` owns browser Bevy WASM startup and diff dispatch for an existing shared project canvas. It initializes the generated `atome/src/wasm/squirrel_bevy_renderer.js` module, prevents starting two Bevy apps for the same canvas, refuses to adopt a started renderer state from a replaced canvas with the same selector, applies surface-size patches through `apply_atome_bevy_surface`, registers the Bevy surface-background runtime, applies `diffVirtualSceneTrees(...)` operations through explicit Bevy WASM exports for spawn, despawn, transform, style, reparent, layer, visibility, text metadata, resource updates, and surface background, and delegates browser media-resource mapping plus presentation scheduling to the owners below; dashboard text nodes that were prewarmed are not rerasterized on transform-only dashboard diffs. It does not own canonical Atome state or provide a legacy renderer fallback. `eVe/domains/rendering/bevy_web_applied_scene_runtime.js` owns the applied-scene baseline: skipped image spawns are omitted until Bevy receives a real spawn, while skipped resource updates preserve the previously applied node so later diffs retry from the visible state. `eVe/domains/rendering/bevy_web_renderer_start_state.js` owns the page-level Bevy event-loop claim and the short-lived starting/ready/failed state factories so concurrent starts on the same canvas coalesce while a second canvas is refused explicitly. A failed startup is retryable only before the Winit runner is invoked; after invocation it becomes terminal for that page/canvas and returns the recorded cause without calling the runner again. The Rust Web renderer mirrors that invariant before Bevy app/plugin construction, preventing both Winit `RecreationAttempt` and duplicate global logger installation. When a project view refresh reuses the same canvas whose Bevy runtime is already started, this owner diffs the last canvas-owned virtual scene against the requested virtual scene and applies the missing render ops instead of returning a passive `already_started` result. Logout must clear project-scene visuals while preserving the shared connected canvas so the Web Bevy event loop remains attached to its real surface.
- The browser Bevy surface-background runtime registration is a post-start lifecycle step: it must run only after the canvas renderer state has been stored with `started: true`, because the background runtime rejects unstarted surfaces instead of buffering against a non-live Bevy app.
- `eVe/domains/rendering/bevy_media_resource_runtime.js` owns browser Bevy media-resource projection for the web runtime. It resolves immediately reusable image/video poster/text/audio-waveform textures before initial projection, keeps uncached video and waveform nodes present with transparent pending material, maps spawn/resource/text texture patches, applies decoded video/waveform textures later through one serialized deferred resource queue, retries deferred video texture failures with bounded backoff when Safari/WKWebView has not produced a frame yet, defers spawned video/waveform textures when metadata is not immediately available, and resumes queued media generation after surface-size patches without introducing a fallback renderer or DOM projection.
- `eVe/domains/rendering/bevy_web_renderer_module_loader.js` owns WASM wrapper import/instantiation and installs the Bevy panic recorder before wasm-bindgen captures `console.error`. `eVe/domains/rendering/bevy_web_presentation_runtime.js` owns browser Bevy presentation scheduling for the web runtime. It filters the known non-fatal WASM runner `unreachable` completion from page error reporting, coalesces every presentation request into one next-frame `request_atome_bevy_redraw`, gates diagnostic-capable WASM startup on initial presentation plus a bounded stability window, coalesces same-tick hidden decode video-frame notifications into one latest-frame WASM notify with a redraw fallback only when no valid notify can be sent, and records WebGPU/canvas context lost/restored events against the existing Bevy canvas without creating a recovery renderer or DOM fallback. `eVe/domains/rendering/bevy_perf_diagnostics_runtime.js` owns shared browser Bevy perf counters, keeps perf collection disabled unless `?perf=1` or an explicit debug flag enables it, and keeps high-frequency external render/video-frame event logging opt-in; hot render loops may gate per-operation events once per batch with `shouldRecordBevyPerfEvent(...)`. `eVe/domains/rendering/bevy_wasm_diagnostics_runtime.js` owns attachment of WASM diagnostics readers to that shared object so the startup/diff owner stays below the critical size threshold and does not duplicate diagnostics glue. The generated Web Bevy runtime in `platforms/web/bevy-renderer/src/lib.rs` uses an opaque canvas surface with Bevy's reactive desktop-app Winit settings, requests an initial redraw before any user input, preserves wake requests made before the winit event-loop proxy is installed, then wakes the Bevy winit event loop and emits redraw requests whenever queued WASM render operations, explicit redraw requests, or hidden video-frame notifications are applied. Video-frame notification application now relies on the existing queued-notification wake instead of adding a second wake after the `RequestRedraw` message. Web diagnostics count wake calls, redraw requests/applies, accepted/applied video-frame redraws, and captured Bevy/WGPU startup panics so startup, Atome creation, mutation diffs, deferred media resource updates, surface background changes, surface-size patches, and video playback present without an idle continuous loop or waiting for a pointer/keyboard event.
- `eVe/domains/rendering/bevy_video_decode_source_runtime.js` owns the hidden browser video decode resources that feed Bevy `GPUExternalTexture`. It synchronizes source-backed video nodes from the Virtual Scene, keeps those videos fully transparent under `#eve_bevy_video_decode_root` (`opacity:0`, 1px, pointer-events none), exposes diagnostics/status lookups including native media errors, applies disposable video timeline projection to the decode element, uses `requestVideoFrameCallback` as the active-playback frame pump when available, and keeps RAF only as fallback: `trimIn` plus `offset` seeking, `trimOut` window enforcement, playback `speed`, and loop handling inside the trimmed source window. The hidden element's source goes through `appendStreamingMediaAuthQuery` (a `<video>` cannot send Authorization; protected recordings need `media_token`), while the entry keeps the projection source. It must not autoplay from scene flags, create visible project media nodes, or become a second video renderer.
- `eVe/domains/rendering/bevy_video_hidden_dom_runtime.js` owns the single fully hidden browser video root and element contract shared by URL decode and ephemeral stream sources. `eVe/domains/rendering/bevy_video_stream_source_runtime.js` owns session-scoped `MediaStream` bindings for Bevy overlay ids: live bindings take priority in the existing `__EVE_BEVY_VIDEO_*` lookups, release an obsolete URL decode element for the same id, emit shared Bevy redraw notifications from `requestVideoFrameCallback` at no more than 15 fps, and dispose their hidden element/callback without stopping capture tracks. These modules are derived renderer resources only; they own no Atome state, visible media node, canvas, recorder, or second renderer.
- `eVe/domains/rendering/surface_size_runtime.js` owns render-surface size derivation from host CSS dimensions, `devicePixelRatio`, coherent `ResizeObserver` device-pixel box data, optional GPU texture clamping, and source-qualified window/visual/native viewport selection. `platforms/desktop-tauri/src/viewport_runtime.rs` publishes the host inner size in logical CSS units at navigation startup and every Tauri resize; it feeds this same size authority through `eve:native-viewport-resize` and creates no renderer or DOM layout owner. Device-pixel boxes that contradict the capped page DPR are ignored so the Bevy backing-store cannot diverge from logical scene coordinates.
- `eVe/domains/rendering/project_scene_runtime.js` owns active project scene projection state keyed by project id. It accepts canonical project Atome records, delegates project-record normalization/filtering to `project_scene_record_projection.js`, delegates client-coordinate hit-test and lasso rectangle collection to `project_scene_hit_testing.js`, builds the disposable Virtual Scene projection once per full render, delegates Bevy native/web renderer execution to `project_scene_bevy_projection_runtime.js`, updates the bounded project render surface, and returns projection handles instead of per-Atome DOM hosts. Foreground renders resolve the canonical `project_view_<projectId>` host when `host` is omitted, then let `surface_runtime.js` reparent the shared `eve_surface_project` canvas there before projection. `project_scene_record_preservation.js` owns the ephemeral overlay preservation rules; `project_scene_direct_transform_runtime.js` owns direct transform patches for pure move/scroll updates; `project_scene_direct_style_runtime.js` owns direct opacity style patches; `project_scene_direct_prefix_runtime.js` owns atomic prefix spawn/despawn and compatible same-id style/geometry updates for hot BevyUI structure changes; and `project_scene_direct_motion_runtime.js` owns transform/style/procedural motion batches. The prefix and motion routes reuse resident material/resources, avoid render-node normalization and RGBA signature hashing on motion frames, support presentable web/native renderers, and serialize through the shared direct-mutation queue in `project_scene_state.js`. Full scene rendering waits for that queue, while a direct operation refuses incompatible foreground, renderer, or scheduler state and falls back to canonical projection. `renderProjectScene(..., { preserveEphemeralRecords: true })` preserves ephemeral Bevy overlay records classified in `project_scene_record_preservation.js` (`mol:lane:`, `mol:clip:`, `mol:kf:`, `mol:playhead`) during late project reconciliation, and preserves dashboard records only while `dashboard_bevy_ui_runtime.js` is active on the same project; closed dashboard records are never preserved through project refresh. On every surface takeover (a scene becoming the canvas owner), `resolvePresentedSceneRecords` carries the still-presented main menu, and the active Dashboard when it leaves or returns to its own scene, into the first submission of the incoming scene, so whichever tree reaches the new scene first cannot present it without them. Visual clearing keeps the previous Virtual Scene until the empty-scene projection has issued Bevy despawns, so the shared canvas cannot keep orphan entities after refresh, logout, or launch transitions. `eVe/intuition/runtime/tool_genesis.js` must await this projection before marking `loadProjectAtomes(...)` complete or refreshing the recent-load cache, so a project refresh cannot cache "loaded" Atomes before Bevy has accepted the scene. `eVe/intuition/runtime/project_layer_geometry_runtime.js` is the single project-layer geometry owner for `project_view_<id>` fullscreen sizing, while `project_layer_visibility_runtime.js` is the single DOM-layer visibility helper used by project loads so only the foreground layer remains interactive above the shared Bevy canvas after project switches or validation reloads.
- `eVe/domains/rendering/project_scene_spatial_index.js` owns the disposable uniform-grid lookup derived from `runtime.scene`; `project_scene_hit_testing.js` uses it to reduce canvas point and rectangle candidates before preserving the canonical hit-test/lasso predicates. `project_scene_incremental_update_runtime.js` applies a compatible single-record WebGPU diff for non-structural style, text, and resource changes, while project ordering, parentage, renderer readiness, background scenes, and queued frames still return to the canonical full projection.
- `eVe/domains/rendering/project_scene_bevy_projection_runtime.js` owns active project Bevy renderer execution. It selects the native or web Bevy runtime according to the existing capability contract, sends startup scenes or diffs, and converts native non-presentable errors into explicit projection results without selecting a Web/WASM fallback on iOS.
- Legacy active project renderer modules `render_at_time.js`, `project_scene_webgpu_adapter.js`, `image_adapter.js`, `video_adapter.js`, `waveform_adapter.js`, `text_adapter.js`, and `project_scene_selection_overlay.js` were removed once the active project route moved to Bevy. Project Atome rendering must use the Bevy Virtual Scene and media texture route, not the old WebGPU adapter family.
- Project selection visuals on the cleaned route are projected by `eVe/domains/rendering/` and drawn by the shared Atome Bevy core: `project_scene_selection_state.js` reads existing selection runtime state, `project_scene_runtime.js` marks disposable render atoms, `virtual_scene_contract.js`/`bevy_projection_adapter.js` propagate `selected` plus dense paint-order render layers, `bevy_web_renderer_runtime.js` forwards those fields in style diffs, `atome/src/shared/render_visual_tokens.js` owns the cross-platform design tokens, and `atome/renderers/bevy-core/src/selection_overlay.rs` draws the selected Atome as a light-gray dotted contour with a progressively faded 12 px shadow blur just above the selected entity and below the next paint layer inside the same project canvas. No per-Atome DOM outline, host class, selection overlay DOM, or legacy WebGPU selection bitmap is allowed on the active project route.
- `eVe/domains/rendering/inline_edit_session.js` owns the pure InlineEditSession state-machine contract: explicit session/project/atom ids, mode, activation source, initial/draft values, focus origin, overlay anchor, `tx_id`, optional gesture id, selection snapshot, immutable open/committed/cancelled transitions, and DOM-authority rejection for session metadata.
- Historical snapshot (retired owner; not current availability evidence): `retired:eVe/domains/rendering/inline_edit_close_overlay.js` owns the pure close-overlay interaction contract for inline editing. It derives disposable overlay metadata from an InlineEditSession, maps close/Escape/Enter/touch/accessibility activation to commit/cancel/none actions, and returns pure focus-restoration data without creating Atomes, DOM nodes, selectors, or visual style state.
- `eVe/domains/rendering/project_scene_runtime.js` owns active project scene orchestration, resize, drag, generic scene-intent commits, and synchronous disposable scene refresh when project-visible records are inserted before the async Bevy frame completes. It delegates live drag/resize frame coalescing to `eVe/domains/rendering/project_scene_gesture_runtime.js`, so move bursts update disposable scene records immediately while WebGPU rerenders and realtime `gesture_frame` commits are batched to animation frames. Text editing is delegated to `eVe/domains/rendering/project_scene_text_runtime.js`, active edit ownership is tracked outside the DOM by `eVe/domains/rendering/project_scene_text_edit_state.js`, and hidden keyboard capture is owned by `eVe/domains/rendering/hidden_text_service_runtime.js`. The hidden service keeps one transparent editor fixed at the bottom edge, with native glyph metrics and a minimum 240 × 24 px surface and 16 px font. Desktop hit testing is transparent; only focused iOS editing keeps the native caret-drag target. It removes `aria-hidden` only while the editor is focusable and owns the transient pre-keyboard viewport lock. `renderProjectScene()` also publishes its caller in the native `scene.start.<atomes>.<proprietaire>` milestone (first frame outside this file, hash suffix stripped, 48 characters max), the only device-visible witness of a render loop. Direct Bevy transform patches are owned by `eVe/domains/rendering/project_scene_direct_transform_runtime.js`, which preserves canonical scale/rotation/origin during live pointer movement, direct opacity/style patches are owned by `eVe/domains/rendering/project_scene_direct_style_runtime.js`, and selection/video/audio invalidation listeners are owned by `eVe/domains/rendering/project_scene_invalidation_runtime.js`. `project_scene_text_runtime.js` consumes `InlineEditSession` for active text edit draft state, focus metadata, overlay anchor metadata, and deterministic `tx_id` propagation while keeping visible text on the Bevy canvas. Text commit and gesture end still persist `set` events through the canonical Atome commit boundary.
- `eVe/domains/rendering/surface_runtime.js` owns centralized project-surface pointer routing plus render-surface host attachment and logical size tracking.
